```
     1→use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};

     4→use crate::options::{PanicMode, StripMode};
     5→
     6→/// Build profile - mutually exclusive
     7→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
     8→#[serde(rename_all = "lowercase")]
     9→pub enum Profile {
    10→    Debug,
    11→    Release,
    12→}
    13→
    14→/// Optimization level - mutually exclusive
    15→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    16→pub enum OptLevel {
    17→    #[serde(rename = "0")]
    18→    O0,
    19→    #[serde(rename = "1")]
    20→    O1,
    21→    #[serde(rename = "2")]
    22→    O2,
    23→    #[serde(rename = "3")]
    24→    O3,
    25→    #[serde(rename = "s")]
    26→    Os,
    27→    #[serde(rename = "z")]
    28→    Oz,
    29→}
    30→
    31→/// Panic strategy - mutually exclusive
    32→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    33→#[serde(rename_all = "kebab-case")]
    34→pub enum PanicStrategy {
    35→    Abort,
    36→    Unwind,
    37→    /// Nightly only: -C panic=immediate-abort (eliminates panic formatting)
    38→    ImmediateAbort,
    39→}
    40→
    41→/// Cargo-specific configuration (flat struct)
    42→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    43→pub struct CargoConfig {
    44→    /// Build profile (debug or release)
    45→    pub profile: Option<Profile>,
    46→    /// Target triple (e.g., "x86_64-unknown-linux-musl")
    47→    pub target_triple: Option<String>,
    48→    /// Custom target JSON file path
    49→    pub target_json: Option<PathBuf>,
    50→    /// Nightly-only -Z flags (e.g., ["panic-immediate-abort"])
    51→    #[serde(default)]
    52→    pub unstable: Vec<String>,
    53→    /// Custom target directory subdirectory for per-spec isolation.
    54→    /// Supports `{name}` (spec filename sans .ts.toml) and `{hash}` (8-char content hash).
    55→    pub target_dir: Option<String>,
    56→}
    57→
    58→/// Rustc codegen and compilation configuration (flat struct)
    59→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    60→pub struct RustcConfig {
    61→    /// Optimization level (-C opt-level=)
    62→    pub opt_level: Option<OptLevel>,
    63→    /// Panic strategy (-C panic=)
    64→    pub panic: Option<PanicStrategy>,
    65→    /// Enable LTO (-C lto=true)
    66→    pub lto: Option<bool>,
    67→    /// Codegen units (-C codegen-units=)
    68→    pub codegen_units: Option<u32>,
    69→    /// Crates to rebuild with -Z build-std (nightly only)
    70→    #[serde(default)]
    71→    pub build_std: Vec<String>,
    72→    /// Raw flags passed through
    73→    #[serde(default)]
    74→    pub flags: Vec<String>,
    75→}
    76→
    77→/// Version script configuration for symbol visibility
    78→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    79→pub struct VersionScript {
    80→    /// Symbols to keep global (e.g., ["_start"])
    81→    pub global: Vec<String>,
    82→    /// Pattern for local symbols (typically "*")
    83→    #[serde(default = "default_local")]
    84→    pub local: String,
    85→}
    86→
    87→fn default_local() -> String {

    89→}
    90→

    93→pub struct LinkerConfig {
```

```
    25→use std::path::Path;
    26→use std::process::ExitCode;
    27→
    28→use crate::find_paths::get_package_name;
    29→
    30→/// Trait for command execution.
    31→pub trait Execute {
    32→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode>;
    33→}
    34→
    35→/// Helper for commands that execute a cargo subcommand.
    36→pub fn execute_cargo_subcommand(
    37→    subcommand: &str,
    38→    args: &[OsString],
    39→    project_root: &Path,
    40→) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    41→    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new("cargo");
    42→    cmd.arg(subcommand);
    43→    cmd.args(args);
    44→    cmd.current_dir(project_root);
    45→    let status = cmd
    46→        .status()
    47→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to run cargo {}", subcommand))?;
    48→    if status.success() {
    49→        Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
    50→    } else {
    51→        bail!("cargo {} failed", subcommand);
    52→    }
    53→}
    54→
    55→/// Check if current directory is a package (has Cargo.toml with [package])
    56→pub(crate) fn current_package_name() -> Option<String> {
    57→    let cwd = std::env::current_dir().ok()?;
    58→    get_package_name(&cwd).ok()
    59→}
    60→
```

```
     1→//! toml_edit helpers for surgical tspec editing that preserves comments/formatting.
     2→
     3→use anyhow::{Result, bail};
     4→use toml_edit::{Array, DocumentMut, Item, Value};
     5→
     6→/// Whether a field holds a scalar or an array.
     7→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
     8→pub enum FieldKind {
     9→    Scalar,
    10→    Array,
    11→}
    12→
    13→/// The set operation to perform.
    14→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
    15→pub enum SetOp {
    16→    /// Replace the field (`=`)
    17→    Replace,
    18→    /// Append to an array (`+=`)
    19→    Append,
    20→    /// Remove from an array (`-=`)
    21→    Remove,
    22→}
    23→
    24→/// Registry entry: (dotted key path, kind).
    25→const FIELD_REGISTRY: &[(&str, FieldKind)] = &[
    26→    ("panic", FieldKind::Scalar),
    27→    ("strip", FieldKind::Scalar),
    28→    ("cargo.profile", FieldKind::Scalar),
    29→    ("cargo.target_triple", FieldKind::Scalar),
    30→    ("cargo.target_json", FieldKind::Scalar),
    31→    ("cargo.target_dir", FieldKind::Scalar),
    32→    ("cargo.unstable", FieldKind::Array),
    33→    ("rustc.opt_level", FieldKind::Scalar),
    34→    ("rustc.panic", FieldKind::Scalar),
    35→    ("rustc.lto", FieldKind::Scalar),
    36→    ("rustc.codegen_units", FieldKind::Scalar),
    37→    ("rustc.build_std", FieldKind::Array),
    38→    ("rustc.flags", FieldKind::Array),
    39→    ("linker.args", FieldKind::Array),
    40→];
    41→
    42→/// Validate that a key is in the registry and return its kind.
    43→pub fn validate_key(key: &str) -> Result<FieldKind> {
    44→    for &(k, kind) in FIELD_REGISTRY {
    45→        if k == key {
    46→            return Ok(kind);
    47→        }
    48→    }
    49→    let valid_keys: Vec<&str> = FIELD_REGISTRY.iter().map(|(k, _)| *k).collect();
    50→    bail!(
    51→        "unknown key: {} (valid keys: {})",
    52→        key,
    53→        valid_keys.join(", ")
    54→    )
    55→}
    56→
    57→/// Validate a value for enum-constrained fields.
    58→/// For unconstrained fields (strings, arrays), accepts anything.
    59→pub fn validate_value(key: &str, value: &str) -> Result<()> {
    60→    match key {
    61→        "panic" => match value {
    62→            "unwind" | "abort" | "immediate-abort" => Ok(()),
    63→            _ => bail!(
    64→                "invalid panic mode: {} (expected: unwind, abort, immediate-abort)",
    65→                value
    66→            ),
    67→        },
    68→        "strip" => match value {
    69→            "none" | "debuginfo" | "symbols" => Ok(()),
    70→            _ => bail!(
    71→                "invalid strip mode: {} (expected: none, debuginfo, symbols)",
    72→                value
    73→            ),
    74→        },
    75→        "cargo.profile" => match value {
    76→            "debug" | "release" => Ok(()),
    77→            _ => bail!("invalid profile: {} (expected: debug, release)", value),
    78→        },
    79→        "rustc.opt_level" => match value {
    80→            "0" | "1" | "2" | "3" | "s" | "z" => Ok(()),
    81→            _ => bail!("invalid opt-level: {} (expected: 0, 1, 2, 3, s, z)", value),
    82→        },
    83→        "rustc.panic" => match value {
    84→            "abort" | "unwind" | "immediate-abort" => Ok(()),
    85→            _ => bail!(
    86→                "invalid panic strategy: {} (expected: abort, unwind, immediate-abort)",
    87→                value
    88→            ),
    89→        },
    90→        "rustc.lto" => match value {
    91→            "true" | "false" | "yes" | "no" | "1" | "0" => Ok(()),
    92→            _ => bail!("invalid boolean: {} (expected: true/false)", value),
    93→        },
    94→        "rustc.codegen_units" => {
    95→            value.parse::<u32>().map_err(|_| {
    96→                anyhow::anyhow!("invalid codegen_units: {} (expected integer)", value)
    97→            })?;
    98→            Ok(())
    99→        }
   100→        _ => Ok(()),
   101→    }
   102→}
   103→
   104→/// Parse a key into (optional table, field).
   105→/// "panic" -> (None, "panic")
   106→/// "rustc.lto" -> (Some("rustc"), "lto")
   107→fn parse_key(key: &str) -> (Option<&str>, &str) {
   108→    match key.split_once('.') {
   109→        Some((table, field)) => (Some(table), field),
   110→        None => (None, key),
   111→    }
   112→}
   113→
   114→/// Parse a value string into a toml_edit Value.
   115→/// Booleans -> bool, integers -> i64, everything else -> string.
   116→fn parse_scalar_value(key: &str, raw: &str) -> Value {
   117→    // For rustc.lto, always parse as boolean
   118→    if key == "rustc.lto" {
   119→        return match raw {
   120→            "true" | "yes" | "1" => Value::from(true),
   121→            _ => Value::from(false),
   122→        };
   123→    }
   124→
   125→    // For rustc.codegen_units, always parse as integer
   126→    if key == "rustc.codegen_units"
   127→        && let Ok(n) = raw.parse::<i64>()
   128→    {
   129→        return Value::from(n);
   130→    }
   131→
   132→    // For rustc.opt_level, keep as string (since "0","1",etc. are enum variants)
   133→    Value::from(raw)
   134→}
   135→
   136→/// Parse an array value from a string.
   137→/// With brackets: `["a","b"]` — parsed as TOML inline array (multiple items).
   138→///   If TOML parse fails, falls back to comma-splitting bare strings: `[a,b]` → `["a","b"]`.
   139→/// Without brackets: treated as a single item (commas are literal).
   140→fn parse_array_value(raw: &str) -> Result<Array> {
   141→    let raw = raw.trim();
   142→
   143→    // Bracket syntax: try TOML first, fall back to bare comma-split
   144→    if raw.starts_with('[') && raw.ends_with(']') {
   145→        let toml_str = format!("x = {}", raw);
   146→        if let Ok(doc) = toml_str.parse::<DocumentMut>()
   147→            && let Some(Item::Value(Value::Array(arr))) = doc.get("x")
   148→        {
   149→            return Ok(arr.clone());
   150→        }
   151→
   152→        // Fallback: treat bracket contents as comma-separated bare strings
   153→        let inner = &raw[1..raw.len() - 1];
   154→        let mut arr = Array::new();
   155→        for item in inner.split(',') {
   156→            let item = item.trim().trim_matches('"').trim_matches('\'');
   157→            if !item.is_empty() {
   158→                arr.push(item);
   159→            }
   160→        }
   161→        return Ok(arr);
   162→    }
   163→
   164→    // No brackets: single item (commas are literal, e.g. "-Wl,--gc-sections")
   165→    let mut arr = Array::new();
   166→    let item = raw.trim_matches('"').trim_matches('\'');
   167→    if !item.is_empty() {
   168→        arr.push(item);
   169→    }
   170→    Ok(arr)
   171→}
   172→
   173→/// Set a field in a toml_edit document.
   174→pub fn set_field(doc: &mut DocumentMut, key: &str, value: &str, kind: FieldKind) -> Result<()> {
   175→    let (table_name, field) = parse_key(key);
   176→
   177→    match kind {
   178→        FieldKind::Scalar => {
   179→            let val = parse_scalar_value(key, value);
   180→            match table_name {
   181→                Some(table) => {
   182→                    // Ensure table exists
   183→                    if doc.get(table).is_none() {
   184→                        doc[table] = toml_edit::Item::Table(toml_edit::Table::new());
   185→                    }
   186→                    doc[table][field] = toml_edit::Item::Value(val);
   187→                }
   188→                None => {
   189→                    doc[field] = toml_edit::Item::Value(val);
   190→                }
   191→            }
   192→        }
   193→        FieldKind::Array => {
   194→            let arr = parse_array_value(value)?;
   195→            match table_name {
   196→                Some(table) => {
   197→                    if doc.get(table).is_none() {
   198→                        doc[table] = toml_edit::Item::Table(toml_edit::Table::new());
   199→                    }
   200→                    doc[table][field] = toml_edit::Item::Value(Value::Array(arr));
   201→                }
   202→                None => {
   203→                    doc[field] = toml_edit::Item::Value(Value::Array(arr));
   204→                }
   205→            }
   206→        }
   207→    }
   208→
   209→    Ok(())
   210→}
   211→
   212→/// Get the existing array for a field, or an empty array if it doesn't exist.
   213→fn get_existing_array(doc: &DocumentMut, key: &str) -> Array {
   214→    let (table_name, field) = parse_key(key);
   215→    match table_name {
   216→        Some(table) => doc
   217→            .get(table)
   218→            .and_then(|t| t.get(field))
   219→            .and_then(|v| v.as_array())
   220→            .cloned()
   221→            .unwrap_or_default(),
   222→        None => doc
   223→            .get(field)
   224→            .and_then(|v| v.as_array())
   225→            .cloned()
   226→            .unwrap_or_default(),
   227→    }
   228→}
   229→
   230→/// Append values to an array field. Creates the array if it doesn't exist.
   231→/// Skips values that are already present (dedup).
   232→pub fn append_field(doc: &mut DocumentMut, key: &str, value: &str) -> Result<()> {
   233→    let (table_name, field) = parse_key(key);
   234→    let mut arr = get_existing_array(doc, key);
   235→    let new_items = parse_array_value(value)?;
   236→
   237→    // Collect existing string values for dedup
   238→    let existing: Vec<String> = arr
   239→        .iter()
   240→        .filter_map(|v| v.as_str().map(|s| s.to_string()))
   241→        .collect();
   242→
   243→    for item in new_items.iter() {
   244→        if let Some(s) = item.as_str()
   245→            && !existing.contains(&s.to_string())
   246→        {
   247→            arr.push(s);
   248→        }
   249→    }
   250→
   251→    match table_name {
   252→        Some(table) => {
   253→            if doc.get(table).is_none() {
   254→                doc[table] = toml_edit::Item::Table(toml_edit::Table::new());
   255→            }
   256→            doc[table][field] = toml_edit::Item::Value(Value::Array(arr));
   257→        }
   258→        None => {
   259→            doc[field] = toml_edit::Item::Value(Value::Array(arr));
   260→        }
   261→    }
   262→
   263→    Ok(())
   264→}
   265→
   266→/// Remove values from an array field. If the array becomes empty, removes the field.
   267→/// If the containing table becomes empty, removes the table too.
   268→pub fn remove_from_field(doc: &mut DocumentMut, key: &str, value: &str) -> Result<()> {
   269→    let (table_name, field) = parse_key(key);
   270→    let arr = get_existing_array(doc, key);
   271→    let to_remove = parse_array_value(value)?;
   272→
   273→    let remove_strs: Vec<String> = to_remove
   274→        .iter()
   275→        .filter_map(|v| v.as_str().map(|s| s.to_string()))
   276→        .collect();
   277→
   278→    let mut new_arr = Array::new();
   279→    for item in arr.iter() {
   280→        if let Some(s) = item.as_str()
   281→            && !remove_strs.contains(&s.to_string())
   282→        {
   283→            new_arr.push(s);
   284→        }
   285→    }
   286→
   287→    if new_arr.is_empty() {
   288→        // Remove the field entirely
   289→        unset_field(doc, key)?;
   290→    } else {
   291→        match table_name {
   292→            Some(table) => {
   293→                if doc.get(table).is_none() {
   294→                    doc[table] = toml_edit::Item::Table(toml_edit::Table::new());
   295→                }
   296→                doc[table][field] = toml_edit::Item::Value(Value::Array(new_arr));
   297→            }
   298→            None => {
   299→                doc[field] = toml_edit::Item::Value(Value::Array(new_arr));
   300→            }
   301→        }
   302→    }
   303→
   304→    Ok(())
   305→}
   306→
   307→/// Remove a field from a toml_edit document.
   308→/// If the containing table becomes empty, remove it too.
   309→pub fn unset_field(doc: &mut DocumentMut, key: &str) -> Result<()> {
   310→    let (table_name, field) = parse_key(key);
   311→
   312→    match table_name {
   313→        Some(table) => {
   314→            if let Some(Item::Table(tbl)) = doc.get_mut(table) {
   315→                tbl.remove(field);
   316→                // If table is now empty, remove it
   317→                if tbl.is_empty() {
   318→                    doc.remove(table);
   319→                }
   320→            }
   321→        }
   322→        None => {
   323→            doc.remove(field);
   324→        }
   325→    }
   326→
   327→    Ok(())
   328→}
```

> AGENT

Here's a summary of the **tspec** codebase:

**tspec v0.9.8** — A spec-driven build system wrapper for Rust that sits on top of cargo. It configures builds at the package level via TOML-based translation spec files (`*.ts.toml`).

### Structure (36 source files)

- **`cli.rs`** — Clap CLI with 10 subcommands: `build`, `run`, `test`, `clean`, `clippy`, `fmt`, `compare`, `ts`, `version`, `install`
- **`cmd/`** — Command implementations using the `Execute` trait. Simple passthroughs (clean, clippy, fmt) use `execute_cargo_subcommand()` helper; others have custom logic.
- **`types.rs`** — Core types: `Spec` with three config sections — `CargoConfig`, `RustcConfig`, `LinkerConfig` — plus top-level `panic` and `strip` options
- **`tspec.rs`** — Spec loading/saving/hashing, `copy_spec_snapshot()` for byte-for-byte backups
- **`ts_cmd/`** — Subcommands for `tspec ts`: `new`, `show`, `list`, `hash`, `set`, `unset`, `backup`, `restore`
- **`ts_cmd/edit.rs`** — `toml_edit`-based surgical editing with field registry, validation, and `set`/`unset`/`append`/`remove` operations
- **`cargo_build.rs`** — Package build orchestration
- **`workspace.rs`** — Workspace package discovery
- **`all.rs`** — Batch operations (`build_all`, `test_all`, `run_all`)

### Three Write Strategies
| Operation | Library | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| load, hash, build | `serde` (`toml`) | Typed `Spec` struct for build logic |
| set, unset | `toml_edit` | Surgical edits preserving comments/formatting |
| backup, restore, new -f | raw `fs::copy` | Exact byte-for-byte preservation |

### Dependencies
`clap`, `serde`, `toml`, `toml_edit`, `anyhow`, `sha2`, `hex`, `cargo_metadata`, `glob` (+ `tempfile` for tests)

### Recent Activity
Last several commits focused on: `ts set`/`unset` improvements using `toml_edit`, array append/remove operators (`+=`/`-=`), shell-safe placeholders (`{}` not `<>`), and bash glob safety analysis.

```
     1→//! `tspec ts set` - Set a value in a tspec using toml_edit (preserves comments/formatting)
     2→
     3→use anyhow::{Context, Result};
     4→use std::path::Path;
     5→use toml_edit::DocumentMut;
     6→
     7→use super::edit::{self, SetOp};
     8→use crate::find_paths::{find_tspec, resolve_package_dir};
     9→
    10→/// Set/append/remove a value in a tspec and save in place
    11→pub fn set_value(
    12→    project_root: &Path,
    13→    package: Option<&str>,
    14→    key: &str,
    15→    value: &str,
    16→    op: SetOp,
    17→    tspec: Option<&str>,
    18→) -> Result<()> {
    19→    let workspace = project_root;
    20→    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(workspace, package)?;
    21→
    22→    // Resolve tspec path (existing or new)
    23→    let output_path = match find_tspec(&package_dir, tspec)? {
    24→        Some(path) => path,
    25→        None => {
    26→            let base_name = match tspec {
    27→                Some(t) => t
    28→                    .strip_suffix(crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX)
    29→                    .or_else(|| t.strip_suffix(".toml"))
    30→                    .unwrap_or(t),
    31→                None => "tspec",
    32→            };
    33→            package_dir.join(format!("{}{}", base_name, crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX))
    34→        }
    35→    };
    36→
    37→    // Validate key and value
    38→    let kind = edit::validate_key(key)?;
    39→
    40→    // Append/remove only make sense for array fields
    41→    if op != SetOp::Replace && kind != edit::FieldKind::Array {
    42→        anyhow::bail!(
    43→            "operator += and -= can only be used with array fields, but '{}' is a scalar",
    44→            key
    45→        );
    46→    }
    47→
    48→    // Only validate enum constraints for replace (append/remove are raw strings)
    49→    if op == SetOp::Replace {
    50→        edit::validate_value(key, value)?;
    51→    }
    52→
    53→    // Read existing content or start empty
    54→    let content = if output_path.exists() {
    55→        std::fs::read_to_string(&output_path)
    56→            .with_context(|| format!("failed to read: {}", output_path.display()))?
    57→    } else {
    58→        String::new()
    59→    };
    60→
    61→    // Parse, edit, write
    62→    let mut doc: DocumentMut = content
    63→        .parse()
    64→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to parse: {}", output_path.display()))?;
    65→
    66→    match op {
    67→        SetOp::Replace => edit::set_field(&mut doc, key, value, kind)?,
    68→        SetOp::Append => edit::append_field(&mut doc, key, value)?,
    69→        SetOp::Remove => edit::remove_from_field(&mut doc, key, value)?,
    70→    }
    71→
    72→    std::fs::write(&output_path, doc.to_string())
    73→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to write: {}", output_path.display()))?;
    74→
    75→    println!(
    76→        "Saved {}",
    77→        output_path
    78→            .strip_prefix(workspace)
    79→            .unwrap_or(&output_path)
    80→            .display()
    81→    );
    82→
    83→    Ok(())
    84→}
```

```
     1→use anyhow::Result;
     2→use clap::{Args, Subcommand};
     3→use std::path::Path;
     4→use std::process::ExitCode;
     5→
     6→use super::Execute;
     7→use crate::ts_cmd::{self, SetOp};
     8→
     9→/// Manage translation specs
    10→#[derive(Args)]
    11→pub struct TsCmd {
    12→    #[command(subcommand)]
    13→    command: TsCommands,
    14→}
    15→
    16→#[derive(Subcommand)]
    17→pub enum TsCommands {
    18→    /// List tspec files in workspace or for a specific package
    19→    List {
    20→        /// Package to list specs for (defaults to current directory or all packages)
    21→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    22→        package: Option<String>,
    23→        /// List all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    24→        #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    25→        all: bool,
    26→    },
    27→    /// Show a tspec's contents
    28→    Show {
    29→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    30→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    31→        package: Option<String>,
    32→        /// Show all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    33→        #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    34→        all: bool,
    35→        /// Tspec name (defaults to all tspec files)
    36→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    37→        tspec: Option<String>,
    38→    },
    39→    /// Show the content hash of a tspec
    40→    Hash {
    41→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    42→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    43→        package: Option<String>,
    44→        /// Hash all workspace packages (even when in a package directory)
    45→        #[arg(short = 'w', long = "workspace")]
    46→        all: bool,
    47→        /// Tspec name (defaults to package's tspec file)
    48→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    49→        tspec: Option<String>,
    50→    },
    51→    /// Create a new tspec file
    52→    New {
    53→        /// Name for the new tspec (default: "tspec")
    54→        #[arg(default_value = "tspec")]
    55→        name: String,
    56→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    57→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    58→        package: Option<String>,
    59→        /// Copy from existing tspec (package/spec or just spec name in same package)
    60→        #[arg(short = 'f', long = "from")]
    61→        from: Option<String>,
    62→    },
    63→    /// Set a value in a tspec (modifies in place, preserves comments)
    64→    ///
    65→    /// Use key=value to replace, key+=value to append to arrays, key-=value to remove from arrays.
    66→    Set {
    67→        /// Assignment: key=value, key+=value (append), or key-=value (remove)
    68→        assignment: String,
    69→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    70→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    71→        package: Option<String>,
    72→        /// Tspec to modify (defaults to package's tspec.ts.toml)
    73→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    74→        tspec: Option<String>,
    75→    },
    76→    /// Remove a field from a tspec (preserves comments)
    77→    Unset {
    78→        /// Key to remove (e.g., "rustc.lto", "panic", "linker.args")
    79→        key: String,
    80→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    81→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    82→        package: Option<String>,
    83→        /// Tspec to modify (defaults to package's tspec.ts.toml)
    84→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    85→        tspec: Option<String>,
    86→    },
    87→    /// Create a versioned backup of a tspec
    88→    Backup {
    89→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    90→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    91→        package: Option<String>,
    92→        /// Tspec to backup (defaults to package's tspec.ts.toml)
    93→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    94→        tspec: Option<String>,
    95→    },
    96→    /// Restore a tspec from a versioned backup
    97→    Restore {
    98→        /// Package name (defaults to current directory)
    99→        #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
   100→        package: Option<String>,
   101→        /// Backup tspec to restore from (required, e.g., "t2-001-abcd1234.ts.toml")
   102→        #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
   103→        tspec: String,
   104→    },
   105→}
   106→
   107→impl Execute for TsCmd {
   108→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode> {
   109→        match &self.command {
   110→            TsCommands::List { package, all } => {
   111→                ts_cmd::list_tspecs(project_root, package.as_deref(), *all)?;
   112→            }
   113→            TsCommands::Show {
   114→                package,
   115→                all,
   116→                tspec,
   117→            } => {
   118→                ts_cmd::show_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), *all, tspec.as_deref())?;
   119→            }
   120→            TsCommands::Hash {
   121→                package,
   122→                all,
   123→                tspec,
   124→            } => {
   125→                ts_cmd::hash_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), *all, tspec.as_deref())?;
   126→            }
   127→            TsCommands::New {
   128→                name,
   129→                package,
   130→                from,
   131→            } => {
   132→                ts_cmd::new_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), name, from.as_deref())?;
   133→            }
   134→            TsCommands::Set {
   135→                assignment,
   136→                package,
   137→                tspec,
   138→            } => {
   139→                let (key, value, op) = parse_assignment(assignment)?;
   140→                ts_cmd::set_value(
   141→                    project_root,
   142→                    package.as_deref(),
   143→                    &key,
   144→                    &value,
   145→                    op,
   146→                    tspec.as_deref(),
   147→                )?;
   148→            }
   149→            TsCommands::Unset {
   150→                key,
   151→                package,
   152→                tspec,
   153→            } => {
   154→                ts_cmd::unset_value(project_root, package.as_deref(), key, tspec.as_deref())?;
   155→            }
   156→            TsCommands::Backup { package, tspec } => {
   157→                ts_cmd::backup_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), tspec.as_deref())?;
   158→            }
   159→            TsCommands::Restore { package, tspec } => {
   160→                ts_cmd::restore_tspec(project_root, package.as_deref(), tspec)?;
   161→            }
   162→        }
   163→        Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   164→    }
   165→}
   166→
   167→/// Parse an assignment string into (key, value, op).
   168→/// Supports: `key=value`, `key+=value`, `key-=value`
   169→fn parse_assignment(assignment: &str) -> Result<(String, String, SetOp)> {
   170→    // Check for += and -= before plain =
   171→    if let Some((key, value)) = assignment.split_once("+=") {
   172→        return Ok((key.to_string(), value.to_string(), SetOp::Append));
   173→    }
   174→    if let Some((key, value)) = assignment.split_once("-=") {
   175→        return Ok((key.to_string(), value.to_string(), SetOp::Remove));
   176→    }
   177→    if let Some((key, value)) = assignment.split_once('=') {
   178→        return Ok((key.to_string(), value.to_string(), SetOp::Replace));
   179→    }
   180→    anyhow::bail!(
   181→        "invalid assignment '{}': expected key=value, key+=value, or key-=value",
   182→        assignment
   183→    )
   184→}
   185→
   186→#[cfg(test)]
   187→mod tests {
   188→    use super::*;
   189→
   190→    #[test]
   191→    fn parse_plain_assignment() {
   192→        let (key, value, op) = parse_assignment("rustc.lto=true").unwrap();
   193→        assert_eq!(key, "rustc.lto");
   194→        assert_eq!(value, "true");
   195→        assert_eq!(op, SetOp::Replace);
   196→    }
   197→
   198→    #[test]
   199→    fn parse_append_assignment() {
   200→        let (key, value, op) = parse_assignment("linker.args+=-Wl,--gc-sections").unwrap();
   201→        assert_eq!(key, "linker.args");
   202→        assert_eq!(value, "-Wl,--gc-sections");
   203→        assert_eq!(op, SetOp::Append);
   204→    }
   205→
   206→    #[test]
   207→    fn parse_remove_assignment() {
   208→        let (key, value, op) = parse_assignment("linker.args-=-static").unwrap();
   209→        assert_eq!(key, "linker.args");
   210→        assert_eq!(value, "-static");
   211→        assert_eq!(op, SetOp::Remove);
   212→    }
   213→
   214→    #[test]
   215→    fn parse_array_bracket_assignment() {
   216→        let (key, value, op) = parse_assignment(r#"linker.args=["-static","-nostdlib"]"#).unwrap();
   217→        assert_eq!(key, "linker.args");
   218→        assert_eq!(value, r#"["-static","-nostdlib"]"#);
   219→        assert_eq!(op, SetOp::Replace);
   220→    }
   221→
   222→    #[test]
   223→    fn parse_no_equals_errors() {
   224→        assert!(parse_assignment("no-equals").is_err());
   225→    }
   226→}
   227→
```

> AGENT

The core problem is that `ts set` takes the entire assignment as **one positional argument** (`key=value`), which forces the user to fight the shell:

```bash
# Simple scalar — fine
tspec ts set rustc.lto=true

# Array — now you're fighting quotes and brackets
tspec ts set 'linker.args=["-static","-nostdlib"]'
#             ^--- shell quote to protect brackets (glob chars)
#                          ^--- inner TOML quotes for strings

# Append — still awkward
tspec ts set 'linker.args+=-Wl,--gc-sections'
```

The fix: **split `key` and `value` into separate clap arguments**, and make `value` variadic for arrays.

### Proposed syntax

```bash
# Scalars — just two words, no quoting ever
tspec ts set rustc.lto true
tspec ts set cargo.profile release
tspec ts set panic abort

# Arrays — each element is its own shell arg (no brackets, no inner quotes)
tspec ts set linker.args -static -nostdlib
tspec ts set linker.args -- -static -nostdlib   # -- if values look like flags

# Append/remove — explicit flags replace the +=/-= encoding
tspec ts set -a linker.args -Wl,--gc-sections
tspec ts set -r linker.args -static
tspec ts set --append linker.args -static -nostdlib
tspec ts set --remove linker.args -static
```

### Why this works

1. **No quoting for scalars** — `key` and `value` are separate shell tokens
2. **No brackets or inner quotes for arrays** — each element is a separate arg; the field registry already knows whether a field is scalar or array, so tspec auto-handles it
3. **No `+=`/`-=` encoded in strings** — `--append`/`--remove` flags are explicit and shell-friendly
4. **`--` escape hatch** — standard POSIX convention for values that look like flags

### The clap change

```rust
Set {
    /// Field key (e.g., "rustc.lto", "linker.args")
    key: String,
    /// Value(s). Multiple values set an array.
    #[arg(required = true)]
    value: Vec<String>,
    /// Append to array instead of replacing
    #[arg(short = 'a', long = "append")]
    append: bool,
    /// Remove from array
    #[arg(short = 'r', long = "remove")]
    remove: bool,
    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
    package: Option<String>,
    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
    tspec: Option<String>,
}
```

Then in the handler: if the field is scalar, `value[0]` is the value. If it's an array, the full `Vec<String>` becomes the array elements. No `parse_assignment()`, no `parse_array_value()` bracket/quote gymnastics.

### Backward compatibility

You could keep the old `key=value` form working by detecting an `=` in the first positional and falling back, but honestly it might be better to just break it cleanly — the old syntax is the problem.

> AGENT

The issue with `set` + flags is that "append to an array" and "set a scalar" aren't the same operation with a mode switch — they're fundamentally different verbs. Making them orthogonal means each operation gets its own command:

### Proposed design

| Command | Purpose | Scope |
|---|---|---|
| `ts set` | Set scalar or replace entire array | whole field |
| `ts unset` | Remove entire field | whole field |
| `ts add` | Add items to array | array items |
| `ts remove` | Remove items from array by value | array items |

`set`/`unset` operate on **fields**. `add`/`remove` operate on **items within arrays**. Two separate axes.

Insert is a modifier on `add` — you're still adding, just at a position:

```bash
# === Field-level (set/unset) ===

# Scalars
tspec ts set rustc.lto true
tspec ts set cargo.profile release

# Replace entire array
tspec ts set linker.args -- -static -nostdlib

# Remove entire field
tspec ts unset linker.args

# === Item-level (add/remove) ===

# Append (default add)
tspec ts add linker.args -- -Wl,--gc-sections
tspec ts add rustc.flags -- -Cforce-frame-pointers=yes -Clink-dead-code=no

# Insert at position
tspec ts add -i 0 linker.args -- -nostdlib

# Remove specific items
tspec ts remove linker.args -- -static
tspec ts remove linker.args -- -static -nostdlib
```

### The five array operations, all distinct

```bash
ts set    linker.args -- -static -nostdlib    # replace all with these
ts unset  linker.args                         # clear the field
ts add    linker.args -- -Wl,--gc-sections    # append
ts add -i 0 linker.args -- -nostdlib          # insert at position
ts remove linker.args -- -static              # remove by value
```

### Clap definitions

```rust
/// Set a field (scalar value or replace entire array)
Set {
    key: String,
    #[arg(required = true)]
    value: Vec<String>,
    ...
}

/// Add items to an array field (append, or insert at position)
Add {
    key: String,
    #[arg(required = true)]
    value: Vec<String>,
    /// Insert at this index instead of appending
    #[arg(short = 'i', long = "index")]
    index: Option<usize>,
    ...
}

/// Remove items from an array field by value
Remove {
    key: String,
    #[arg(required = true)]
    value: Vec<String>,
    ...
}

/// Remove a field entirely (already exists)
Unset { key: String, ... }
```

### Why this is orthogonal

- **No flags that change the fundamental operation** — each verb does one thing
- **`add` and `remove` are a natural pair** — symmetric item-level operations
- **`set` and `unset` are a natural pair** — symmetric field-level operations
- **`--index` on `add` is the only modifier**, and it doesn't change *what* it does (add items), only *where*
- **`add` deduplicates** — if the item's already there, no-op. Want duplicates? Use `set` to replace the whole array

LGTM, but also allow --index for remove so it can remove by index or value, thoughts

> AGENT

Makes sense — it's symmetric. `-i` means "at this position" for both `add` and `remove`:

```bash
# add: what to insert (always need values)
ts add    linker.args -- -static          # append at end
ts add -i 0 linker.args -- -static        # insert at index 0

# remove: what to remove (value OR position, not both)
ts remove linker.args -- -static          # remove by value
ts remove -i 2 linker.args               # remove item at index 2
```

The difference: `add -i` still requires values (what to insert at that position). `remove -i` doesn't (the index *is* the selector, so `value` is empty).

```rust
Remove {
    key: String,
    /// Items to remove by value (not used with --index)
    value: Vec<String>,              // NOT required
    /// Remove item at this index instead of by value
    #[arg(short = 'i', long = "index")]
    index: Option<usize>,
    ...
}
```

Validation is simple — exactly one selector:
- `--index` provided, no values → remove by position
- Values provided, no `--index` → remove by value
- Both or neither → error

One consideration: removing by index could support multiple indices (`-i 0 -i 3`), but then you have to apply them highest-first to avoid index shifting. I'd keep it to a single index per invocation — simple, no surprises. If you need to remove multiple by index, run it twice from the end.

     5→## 20260209 - ts set/unset/backup/restore improvements using toml_edit
     6→
     7→### Context
     8→
     9→`ts set` currently only supports ~10 hardcoded scalar keys and silently ignores all array/nested fields (`rustc.build_std`, `rustc.flags`, `linker.args`, `cargo.unstable`, `cargo.target_json`, `linker.version_script`). Users get `Error: unknown key` for documented fields. Additionally, `ts backup`, `ts restore`, and `ts new -f` round-trip through serde serialization, which destroys user comments and formatting.
    10→
    11→### Three write strategies
    12→
    13→| Operation | Strategy | Why |
    14→|---|---|---|
    15→| load, hash, build | serde (`toml`) | Need typed `Spec` struct for build logic |
    16→| set, unset | `toml_edit` (`DocumentMut`) | Surgical edit preserving comments/formatting |
    17→| backup, restore, new -f | raw `fs::copy` | Exact byte-for-byte preservation |

    33→  ("cargo.target_json", Scalar) ("cargo.target_dir", Scalar)

    38→  ```

Value parsing: booleans as `toml_edit::value(true/false)`, integers as `toml_edit::value(i64)`, everything else as string. For arrays: parse `[a,b,c]` or `"a","b"` syntax -> replace the array.

   164→
   165→## 20260210 - Shell-hostile characters in values and array syntax
   166→
   167→### Problem
   168→
   169→Several tspec features used characters that conflict with bash:
   170→
   171→1. **`<name>`/`<hash>` placeholders in `cargo.target_dir`** — `<` and `>` are shell
   172→   redirection operators, so `tspec ts set cargo.target_dir=<name>-xyz` triggers
   173→   `bash: name: No such file or directory`. Users must quote the whole argument.
   174→
   175→2. **Bracket array syntax with unquoted values** — `tspec ts set linker.args-=[za]`
   176→   fails because `parse_array_value` tries to parse `[za]` as a TOML inline array,
   177→   and bare `za` is not valid TOML (must be `["za"]`). Meanwhile `linker.args+=za`
   178→   (no brackets) works fine since the bare path treats it as a single string.
   179→
   180→3. **Shell quote stripping compounds the bracket problem** — even
   181→   `tspec ts set linker.args-=["za"]` fails because bash strips the double quotes,
   182→   passing `[za]` to tspec. Only single-quoting the entire argument works:
   183→   `tspec ts set 'linker.args-=["za"]'`.
   184→
   185→### Fix 1: `<>` → `{}` placeholders (v0.9.8)
   186→
   187→Replaced `<name>`/`<hash>` with `{name}`/`{hash}` in `expand_target_dir()`. Curly
   188→braces are shell-inert — bash brace expansion only triggers with commas (`{a,b}`) or
   189→ranges (`{1..3}`) inside, so `{name}` passes through untouched.
   190→
   191→**Files changed:** `src/tspec.rs`, `src/types.rs`, `src/ts_cmd/set.rs` (test),
   192→`README.md`
   193→
   194→### Fix 2: Bare-string fallback for bracket array syntax (v0.9.8)
   195→
   196→Changed `parse_array_value()` to try TOML parsing first, then fall back to
   197→comma-splitting bare strings when TOML fails. This means:
   198→
   199→| Input | Parse path | Result |
   200→|---|---|---|
   201→| `za` | bare (no brackets) | single item `"za"` |
   202→| `[za]` | TOML fails → bare fallback | single item `"za"` |
   203→| `[-static,-nostdlib]` | TOML fails → bare fallback | two items |
   204→| `[-static, -nostdlib]` | TOML fails → bare fallback | two items (spaces trimmed) |
   205→| `["-Wl,--gc-sections", "-static"]` | TOML succeeds | two items (embedded comma preserved) |
   206→
   207→The TOML-first path is preserved so that properly quoted strings with embedded commas
   208→still work — `"-Wl,--gc-sections"` contains a comma that must not be split on.
   209→
   210→**Files changed:** `src/ts_cmd/edit.rs` (7 new tests)
   211→
   212→### Bash glob safety of square brackets
   213→
   214→Square brackets are bash glob characters — `[fx]` matches a single-character filename
   215→from the set {f, x}. Initial concern was that `linker.args+=[-static,-nostdlib]` could
   216→be glob-expanded. Investigation showed this is safe because **bash globs the entire
   217→token**, not just the bracket portion. The token `linker.args+=[-static,-nostdlib]`
   218→would only match a file literally named `linker.args+=-` (or `s`, `t`, etc.), which
   219→will never exist.
   220→
   221→Standalone brackets DO expand:
   222→
   223→```bash
   224→$ echo [fx]          # no f or x file
   225→[fx]
   226→$ touch f
   227→$ echo [fx]          # f exists
   228→f
   229→$ touch x
   230→$ echo [fx]          # both exist
   231→f x
   232→$ rm f
   233→$ echo [fx]          # only x
   234→x
   235→$ rm x
   236→$ echo [fx]          # neither exists, passes through
   237→[fx]
   238→```
   239→
   240→But with a prefix, the whole token fails to match any file:
   241→
   242→```bash
   243→$ touch f x
   244→$ echo [fx]          # standalone: expands
   245→f x
   246→$ echo a=[fx]        # prefixed: no file matches "a=f" or "a=x"
   247→a=[fx]
   248→```
   249→
   250→**Conclusion:** Square brackets are safe unquoted in `tspec ts set key+=[values]`
   251→syntax. No quoting needed for normal use.
   252→
```

     1→# Chores-2
     2→
     3→Continued maintenance tasks, following on from chores-1.
     4→
     5→## 20260206 - Ts Execute Conversion
     6→
     7→### Convert `Commands::Ts` to use Execute trait
     8→
     9→The `Ts` variant in `Commands` is the only command that doesn't use the `Execute` trait. All other commands (Build, Run, Test, Clean, Clippy, Fmt, Compare, Version, Install) follow the pattern where a command struct implements `Execute` and main.rs calls `cmd.execute(&project_root)`. The `Ts` subcommands instead destructure fields in main.rs and call free functions from `ts_cmd::*`.
    10→
    11→**Current state in main.rs:**
    12→```rust
    13→Commands::Ts { command } => match command {
    14→    TsCommands::List { package, all } => {
    15→        ts_cmd::list_tspecs(package.as_deref(), all)?;
    16→    }
    17→    TsCommands::Show { package, all, tspec } => {
    18→        ts_cmd::show_tspec(package.as_deref(), all, tspec.as_deref())?;
    19→    }
    20→    TsCommands::Hash { package, all, tspec } => {
    21→        ts_cmd::hash_tspec(package.as_deref(), all, tspec.as_deref())?;
    22→    }
    23→    TsCommands::New { name, package, from } => {
    24→        ts_cmd::new_tspec(package.as_deref(), &name, from.as_deref())?;
    25→    }
    26→    TsCommands::Set { assignment, package, tspec } => {
    27→        let (key, value) = assignment.split_once('=')...;
    28→        ts_cmd::set_value(package.as_deref(), key, value, tspec.as_deref())?;
    29→    }
    30→},
    31→```
    32→
    33→**Goal state in main.rs:**
    34→```rust
    35→Commands::Ts(cmd) => {
    36→    cmd.execute(&find_project_root()?)?;
    37→}
    38→```
    39→
    40→**Implementation steps:**
    41→
    42→1. **Create `src/cmd/ts.rs`** with a `TsCmd` struct that wraps `TsCommands`:
    43→   ```rust
    44→   #[derive(Args)]
    45→   pub struct TsCmd {
    46→       #[command(subcommand)]
    47→       command: TsCommands,
    48→   }
    49→
    50→   impl Execute for TsCmd {
    51→       fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    52→           match &self.command {
    53→               TsCommands::List { package, all } => {
    54→                   ts_cmd::list_tspecs(package.as_deref(), *all)?;
    55→               }
    56→               // ... other variants
    57→           }
    58→           Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
    59→       }
    60→   }
    61→   ```
    62→
    63→2. **Move `TsCommands` enum** from `cli.rs` to `src/cmd/ts.rs` (or keep in cli.rs and import — either works, but co-locating with the Execute impl is cleaner).
    64→
    65→3. **Update `cli.rs`** to use `Ts(TsCmd)` tuple variant instead of `Ts { command: TsCommands }` struct variant:
    66→   ```rust
    67→   // Before
    68→   Ts {
    69→       #[command(subcommand)]
    70→       command: TsCommands,
    71→   },
    72→   // After
    73→   Ts(TsCmd),
    74→   ```
    75→
    76→4. **Update `src/cmd/mod.rs`** to add `mod ts;` and `pub use ts::TsCmd;`.
    77→
    78→5. **Update main.rs** to replace the 30-line `Ts` match arm with the one-liner.
    79→
    80→6. **Consider whether `ts_cmd/` functions need `project_root`** — currently they call `find_project_root()` internally or use `current_dir()`. The Execute trait passes `project_root`, so the ts_cmd functions could be updated to accept it instead of finding it themselves. This is optional but would make them consistent.

    91→## 20260206 - Add `cargo.target_dir` spec field
    92→
    93→### The Problem
    94→
    95→When two specs share the same target triple, builds overwrite each other in `target/{triple}/{profile}/`. This makes `tspec compare` unreliable and prevents side-by-side builds.
    96→
    97→### The Design
    98→
    99→Add a `target_dir` field to the `[cargo]` spec section with template placeholder support:
   100→
   101→```toml
   102→[cargo]
   103→target_dir = "<name>"           # spec filename sans .ts.toml
   104→target_dir = "<hash>"           # 8-char content hash
   105→target_dir = "<name>-<hash>"    # combined
   106→target_dir = ""                 # empty = no subdir (backward compat)
   107→```
   108→
   109→Path structure uses cargo's native `--target-dir`: `target/{target_dir}/{triple}/{profile}/{binary}`
   110→
   111→Default when field absent: empty (backward compatible, no subdirectory).
   112→
   113→### The Plan
   114→
   115→The `target_dir` field threads through the build pipeline: types → spec loading → path resolution → cargo command → binary scanning. The double `load_spec` call in `build_crate` gets consolidated along the way.
   116→
   117→1. **Add `target_dir` field to `CargoConfig`** (`src/types.rs`)
   118→   - Add `pub target_dir: Option<String>` to `CargoConfig`
   119→
   120→2. **Add helpers in `src/tspec.rs`**
   121→   - `spec_name_from_path(path: &Path) -> String` — strips `.ts.toml` suffix from filename
   122→   - `expand_target_dir(spec: &Spec, spec_name: &str) -> Result<Option<String>>` — expands `<name>` and `<hash>` placeholders; returns `None` if field is absent or empty
   123→
   124→3. **Update `get_binary_path`** (`src/find_paths.rs`)
   125→   - Add `expanded_target_dir: Option<&str>` parameter
   126→   - When `Some(td)`, base path becomes `workspace.join("target").join(td)` instead of `workspace.join("target")`
   127→   - Update existing test call sites to pass `None`
   128→
   129→4. **Update `apply_spec_to_command`** (`src/cargo_build.rs`)
   130→   - Add `expanded_target_dir: Option<&str>` parameter
   131→   - When `Some(td)`, insert `--target-dir target/{td}` into cargo command
   132→   - Fix version script path to use expanded target dir
   133→
   134→5. **Refactor `build_crate`** (`src/cargo_build.rs`)
   135→   - Consolidate the double `load_spec` call into one
   136→   - Compute `spec_name` and `expanded_td` after loading
   137→   - Pass `expanded_td` to both `get_binary_path` and `apply_spec_to_command`
   138→   - Add `target_base: PathBuf` to `BuildResult`
   139→
   140→6. **Update `test_crate`** (`src/testing.rs`)
   141→   - Compute `expanded_td` from loaded spec and pass to `apply_spec_to_command`
   142→
   143→7. **Fix test binary scanning** (`src/all.rs`)
   144→   - Capture `BuildResult` from `build_crate` (currently only error case captured)
   145→   - Use `build_result.target_base.join(profile)` instead of hardcoded `workspace.root.join("target").join(profile)`
   146→
   147→8. **Add `cargo.target_dir` to `tspec ts set`** (`src/ts_cmd/set.rs`)
   148→   - Add match arm for `"cargo.target_dir"` in `apply_value`
   149→
   150→9. **Tests**
   151→   - Unit tests for `expand_target_dir` (None, empty, literal, `<name>`, `<hash>`, combined)
   152→   - Unit tests for `spec_name_from_path`
   153→   - Unit test for `get_binary_path` with target_dir
   154→   - Unit test for `apply_value` with `cargo.target_dir`
   155→   - Existing tests: append `None` to `get_binary_path` calls (mechanical)
   156→
   157→**Status:** Done
   158→
   159→## 20260207 - In-place `set`, add `backup` and `restore` subcommands
   160→
   161→### The Problem
   162→
   163→`tspec ts set` creates a new snapshot file (`name-NNN-hash.ts.toml`) on every edit.
   164→Chaining edits compounds the names: `t1-001-xxx-001-yyy.ts.toml`. The file you work
   165→with should keep its name; backup/restore should be explicit user actions.
   166→
   167→### The Design
   168→
   169→1. **`tspec ts set`** modifies the file in place (no snapshot creation)
   170→2. **`tspec ts backup -t t1.ts.toml`** creates `t1-001-<hash>.ts.toml`
   171→3. **`tspec ts restore -t t1-001-<hash>.ts.toml`** copies it back to `t1.ts.toml`
   172→4. Backups are valid specs — `tspec build -t t1-001-<hash>.ts.toml` works directly
   173→
   174→### The Plan
   175→
   176→#### 1. Modify `tspec ts set` to save in place
   177→
   178→**File:** `src/ts_cmd/set.rs`
   179→
   180→- Change `set_value()` to save back to the original file path instead of calling `save_spec_snapshot()`
   181→- If the file existed (found via `find_tspec()`), save to that path
   182→- If no file existed, construct path from `-t` arg (or default `tspec`) + `TSPEC_SUFFIX` in package dir
   183→- Use existing `save_spec()` instead of `save_spec_snapshot()`
   184→- Remove `save_spec_snapshot` import
   185→
   186→**File:** `src/cmd/ts.rs`
   187→- Update `Set` variant doc comment from "creates versioned copy" to "modifies in place"
   188→
   189→#### 2. Add `tspec ts backup` subcommand
   190→
   191→**New file:** `src/ts_cmd/backup.rs`
   192→
   193→```rust
   194→pub fn backup_tspec(project_root, package, tspec) -> Result<()>
   195→```
   196→
   197→- Locate the spec via `find_tspec()`; error if not found
   198→- Extract base name via `spec_name_from_path()`
   199→- Load the spec, call existing `save_spec_snapshot()` to create `{name}-NNN-{hash}.ts.toml`
   200→- Print the backup filename
   201→
   202→**File:** `src/cmd/ts.rs` — add `Backup` variant with `-p` and `-t` args, dispatch
   203→**File:** `src/ts_cmd/mod.rs` — add `mod backup;` and `pub use backup::backup_tspec;`
   204→
   205→#### 3. Add `tspec ts restore` subcommand
   206→
   207→**New file:** `src/ts_cmd/restore.rs`
   208→
   209→```rust
   210→pub fn restore_tspec(project_root, package, tspec) -> Result<()>
   211→```
   212→
   213→- Locate the backup file via `find_tspec()` (`-t` required)
   214→- Parse the filename to extract the base name by stripping trailing `-NNN-HHHHHHHH`
   215→  (3-digit seq + 8-char hex hash) from the stem before `.ts.toml`
   216→- Construct target path: `{base_name}.ts.toml` in the same directory
   217→- Copy content to the base file using `load_spec()` + `save_spec()`
   218→- Print what was restored
   219→
   220→**File:** `src/cmd/ts.rs` — add `Restore` variant with `-p` and `-t` (required) args, dispatch
   221→**File:** `src/ts_cmd/mod.rs` — add `mod restore;` and `pub use restore::restore_tspec;`
   222→
   223→### Files modified
   224→- `src/ts_cmd/set.rs` — save in place instead of snapshot
   225→- `src/cmd/ts.rs` — add Backup/Restore variants, update Set doc
   226→- `src/ts_cmd/mod.rs` — register new modules
   227→
   228→### Files created
   229→- `src/ts_cmd/backup.rs` — backup logic
   230→- `src/ts_cmd/restore.rs` — restore logic (with name-suffix parsing)
   231→
   232→### Verification
   233→```bash
   234→tspec test -p tspec
   235→tspec clippy
   236→tspec fmt --check
   237→
   238→# Manual smoke test:
   239→tspec ts new t2
   240→tspec ts set strip=symbols -t t2.ts.toml      # modifies t2.ts.toml in place
   241→tspec ts show -t t2.ts.toml                    # shows strip = "symbols"
   242→tspec ts backup -t t2.ts.toml                  # creates t2-001-<hash>.ts.toml
   243→tspec ts set panic=abort -t t2.ts.toml         # modifies t2.ts.toml again
   244→tspec ts show -t t2.ts.toml                    # shows panic + strip
   245→tspec ts restore -t t2-001-<hash>.ts.toml      # restores t2.ts.toml to pre-panic state
   246→tspec build -t t2-001-<hash>.ts.toml           # backup usable directly
   247→```
   248→
   249→**Status:** Done
   250→
   251→## 20260208 - Rename `--all` to `--workspace`
   252→
   253→### The Problem
   254→
   255→`--all` (`-a`) appears on build, run, test, clippy, and fmt. It means "all packages
   256→in the workspace". Several issues:
   257→
   258→1. **POPs:** For a single-package project, `--all` was silently doing nothing because
   259→   `classify_crate()` classified the lone crate as `BuildTool` (either by name match
   260→   or by the fallback for crates not in `apps/`, `libs/`, or `tools/` directories).
   261→   Fixed by treating POPs as `App` unconditionally in `WorkspaceInfo::discover()`.
   262→
   263→2. **Ambiguity with two dimensions:** Currently `--all` means "all packages". But
   264→   tspec has two axes — packages and specs. When you want "build all specs for all
   265→   packages" or "build all specs for one package", `--all` doesn't say which axis.
   266→   Future options:
   267→   - `--all-packages` / `--all-specs` (explicit, verbose)
   268→   - `--all` means both axes, with separate flags for each
   269→   - Positional/glob patterns for specs (e.g., `tspec build -p myapp 'tspec*.ts.toml'`)
   270→
   271→3. **Workspace + cwd:** When inside a package directory in a workspace, `--all`
   272→   overrides the cwd default and builds all workspace members. Without `--all`, it
   273→   builds just the cwd package. This is correct but non-obvious.
   274→
   275→### Quick Fix (done)
   276→
   277→POPs now classify their single crate as `App` so `--all` includes it. No changes
   278→to workspace behavior.
   279→
   280→### Resolution
   281→
   282→Cargo itself deprecated `--all` in favor of `--workspace`. We do the same:
   283→rename `--all` / `-a` to `--workspace` / `-w` across all commands (build, run,
   284→test, clippy, fmt, and ts subcommands).
   285→
   286→### The Plan
   287→
   288→Mechanical rename in 8 locations across 7 files:
   289→
   290→**`src/cmd/build.rs`** — BuildCmd: `--all`/`-a` → `--workspace`/`-w`
   291→**`src/cmd/test.rs`** — TestCmd: same
   292→**`src/cmd/run.rs`** — RunCmd: same
   293→**`src/cmd/clippy.rs`** — ClippyCmd: same (already passes `--workspace` to cargo)
   294→**`src/cmd/fmt.rs`** — FmtCmd: same (still passes `--all` to `cargo fmt` which uses that name)
   295→**`src/cmd/ts.rs`** — TsCommands::List, Show, Hash: same
   296→
   297→Also rename the struct field from `all` to `workspace` in each.
   298→
   299→**`README.md`** — Update usage examples from `-a`/`--all` to `-w`/`--workspace`
   300→
   301→**Status:** Done
   302→
```

id: toolu_019txSed6PoVmMAKTHgbwmkV
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/notes/chores-3.md",
  "old_string": "**Conclusion:** Square brackets are safe unquoted in `tspec ts set key+=[values]`\nsyntax. No quoting needed for normal use.",
  "new_string": "**Conclusion:** Square brackets are safe unquoted in `tspec ts set key+=[values]`\nsyntax. No quoting needed for normal use.\n\n## 20260211 - Orthogonal `ts set`/`add`/`remove` with separate key and value args\n\n### Problem\n\n`ts set` takes the entire assignment as a single positional argument (`key=value`),\nwhich forces users to fight the shell:\n\n```bash\n# Scalar — fine\ntspec ts set rustc.lto=true\n\n# Array — bracket/quote gymnastics\ntspec ts set 'linker.args=[\"-static\",\"-nostdlib\"]'\n\n# Append — still awkward\ntspec ts set 'linker.args+=-Wl,--gc-sections'\n```\n\nThe root cause is two-fold:\n\n1. **Key and value crammed into one shell token** — any special characters in\n   the value (brackets, quotes, spaces) force quoting the entire `key=value`.\n2. **Overloaded operators (`=`, `+=`, `-=`) encoded in a string** — three\n   fundamentally different operations (replace, append, remove) masquerade as\n   modes of one command via string parsing.\n\n### Design: Orthogonal verbs with separate args\n\nSplit into four commands on two axes:\n\n| Axis | Command | Purpose |\n|---|---|---|\n| Field-level | `ts set` | Set scalar or replace entire array |\n| Field-level | `ts unset` | Remove entire field (already exists) |\n| Item-level | `ts add` | Add items to array (append or insert) |\n| Item-level | `ts remove` | Remove items from array (by value or index) |\n\nKey and value are separate clap positional arguments. Array values are variadic\n(each element is its own shell arg — no brackets, no inner quotes).\n\n### Syntax\n\n```bash\n# === Field-level (set/unset) ===\n\n# Scalars — just two words\ntspec ts set rustc.lto true\ntspec ts set cargo.profile release\ntspec ts set panic abort\n\n# Replace entire array — each element is a separate arg\ntspec ts set linker.args -- -static -nostdlib\ntspec ts set rustc.build_std core alloc\n\n# Remove entire field\ntspec ts unset linker.args\n\n# === Item-level (add/remove) ===\n\n# Append (default add behavior)\ntspec ts add linker.args -- -Wl,--gc-sections\ntspec ts add rustc.flags -- -Cforce-frame-pointers=yes -Clink-dead-code=no\n\n# Insert at position\ntspec ts add -i 0 linker.args -- -nostdlib\n\n# Remove by value\ntspec ts remove linker.args -- -static\ntspec ts remove linker.args -- -static -nostdlib\n\n# Remove by index\ntspec ts remove -i 2 linker.args\n```\n\n### The five array operations\n\n| # | Operation | Command | Example |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | Replace all | `ts set` | `ts set linker.args -- -static -nostdlib` |\n| 2 | Clear field | `ts unset` | `ts unset linker.args` |\n| 3 | Append items | `ts add` | `ts add linker.args -- -Wl,--gc-sections` |\n| 4 | Insert at position | `ts add -i N` | `ts add -i 0 linker.args -- -nostdlib` |\n| 5 | Remove by value | `ts remove` | `ts remove linker.args -- -static` |\n| 6 | Remove by index | `ts remove -i N` | `ts remove -i 2 linker.args` |\n\n### Clap definitions\n\n```rust\n/// Set a field (scalar value or replace entire array)\nSet {\n    /// Field key (e.g., \"rustc.lto\", \"linker.args\")\n    key: String,\n    /// Value(s). For scalars, one value. For arrays, each arg is an element.\n    #[arg(required = true)]\n    value: Vec<String>,\n    #[arg(short = 'p', long = \"package\")]\n    package: Option<String>,\n    #[arg(short = 't', long = \"tspec\")]\n    tspec: Option<String>,\n}\n\n/// Add items to an array field (append by default, or insert at position)\nAdd {\n    /// Field key (must be an array field)\n    key: String,\n    /// Items to add\n    #[arg(required = true)]\n    value: Vec<String>,\n    /// Insert at this index instead of appending\n    #[arg(short = 'i', long = \"index\")]\n    index: Option<usize>,\n    #[arg(short = 'p', long = \"package\")]\n    package: Option<String>,\n    #[arg(short = 't', long = \"tspec\")]\n    tspec: Option<String>,\n}\n\n/// Remove items from an array field (by value or by index)\nRemove {\n    /// Field key (must be an array field)\n    key: String,\n    /// Items to remove by value (not used with --index)\n    value: Vec<String>,\n    /// Remove item at this index instead of by value\n    #[arg(short = 'i', long = \"index\")]\n    index: Option<usize>,\n    #[arg(short = 'p', long = \"package\")]\n    package: Option<String>,\n    #[arg(short = 't', long = \"tspec\")]\n    tspec: Option<String>,\n}\n```\n\n### Validation rules\n\n- `ts set` on a scalar field: exactly one value required\n- `ts set` on an array field: one or more values (replaces entire array)\n- `ts add`: key must be an array field (error on scalar)\n- `ts add -i N`: index must be ≤ current array length\n- `ts remove` without `--index`: at least one value required\n- `ts remove -i parsing), with\n  optional insert-at-index. Deduplicates on append; for insert, adds at position\n  without dedup (user explicitly chose the position).\n\n- **`remove_items_by_value(doc, key, values: &[String])`** — like\n  `remove_from_field` but takes `Vec<String>` directly. If array becomes empty,\n  removes the field (existing behavior).\n\n- **`remove_item_by_index(doc, key, index: usize)`** — removes the item at\n  the given index. If array becomes empty, removes the field.\n\n- **`set_field_from_strings(doc, key, values: &[String], kind)`** — for `ts set`.\n  Scalars: `values[0]`. Arrays: builds array from all values. Replaces\n  `set_field(doc, key, value_str, kind)` which parsed from a single string.\n\nKeep `parse_array_value()` and `parse_scalar_value()` as internal helpers but\nthe public API takes `&[String]` — the shell already did the splitting.\n\n#### Step 2: Add `add_value` and `remove_value` to `ts_cmd/`\n\n**New file:** `src/ts_cmd/add.rs`\n\n```rust\npub fn add_value(\n    project_root: &Path,\n    package: Option<&str>,\n    key: &str,\n    values: &[String],\n    index: Option<usize>,\n    tspec: Option<&str>,\n) -> Result<()>\n```\n\n- Validate key is an array field (error if scalar)\n- Read, parse DocumentMut, call `edit::add_items()`, write back\n\n**New file:** `src/ts_cmd/remove.rs`\n\n```rust\npub fn remove_value(\n    project_root: &Path,\n    package: Option<&str>,\n    key: &str,\n    values: &[String],\n    index: Option<usize>,\n    tspec: Option<&str>,\n) -> Result<()>\n```\n\n- Validate key is an array field (error if scalar)\n- If `index` is Some: call `edit::remove_item_by_index()`\n- If `index` is None and values non-empty: call `edit::remove_items_by_value()`\n- If neither: error\n\n#### Step 3: Rewrite `set_value` to accept `&[String]`\n\n**File:** `src/ts_cmd/set.rs`\n\n- Change signature: `value: &str` → `values: &[String]`\n- For scalar fields: validate `values.len() == 1`, then use `values[0]`\n- For array fields: pass all values to `edit::set_field_from_strings()`\n- Remove `parse_assignment()` from `src/cmd/ts.rs`\n\n#### Step 4: Wire up `Add` and `Remove` in CLI\n\n**File:** `src/cmd/ts.rs`\n\n- Add `Add` and `Remove` variants to `TsCommands`\n- Add match arms in `TsCmd::execute()`\n- Remove `parse_assignment()` (no longer needed)\n- Update `Set` variant: `assignment: String` → `key: String` + `value: Vec<String>`\n\n**File:** `src/ts_cmd/mod.rs`\n\n- Add `mod add; > DEVELOPER
```

but I don't think the `--` is necessary since the ts sub-command is builtin not spawned, thoughts.

> AGENT

Good instinct, but the issue isn't the shell — it's **clap's argument parser**. When clap sees:

```bash
tspec ts set linker.args -static -nostdlib
```

It tries to parse `-static` as flags: `-s`, `-t` (matches `--tspec`!), `-a`, `-t`, `-i`, `-c`. This happens regardless of whether the command is builtin or spawned.

**However**, clap has `allow_hyphen_values = true` which solves this:

```rust
/// Value(s). For scalars, one value. For arrays, each arg is an element.
#[arg(required = true, allow_hyphen_values = true)]
value: Vec<String>,
```

With this, clap treats unknown hyphen-prefixed tokens as positional values instead of trying to parse them as flags. So `-static`, `-nostdlib`, `-Wl,--gc-sections` all just work.

The only edge case: if a value exactly collides with a **defined** short flag (`-p`, `-t`, `-i`), clap still consumes it as that flag. In practice this never happens — no real linker/rustc flag is just `-p` or `-t`. But `--` remains available as an escape hatch for that corner case.

So the examples simplify to:

```bash
tspec ts set linker.args -static -nostdlib        # just works
tspec ts add linker.args -Wl,--gc-sections        # just works
tspec ts remove linker.args -static               # just works
```

No `--` in docs, no `--` in normal use. I'll update the plan to reflect this — add `allow_hyphen_values = true` and mention `--` only as a footnote for the theoretical collision case.

Want me to update chores-3 with this correction, then do the todo + version bump?

   252→
   253→## 20260211 - Orthogonal `ts set`/`add`/`remove` with separate key and value args
   254→
   255→### Problem
   256→
   257→`ts set` takes the entire assignment as a single positional argument (`key=value`),
   258→which forces users to fight the shell:
   259→
   260→```bash
   261→# Scalar — fine
   262→tspec ts set rustc.lto=true
   263→
   264→# Array — bracket/quote gymnastics
   265→tspec ts set 'linker.args=["-static","-nostdlib"]'
   266→
   267→# Append — still awkward
   268→tspec ts set 'linker.args+=-Wl,--gc-sections'
   269→```
   270→
   271→The root cause is two-fold:
   272→
   273→1. **Key and value crammed into one shell token** — any special characters in
   274→   the value (brackets, quotes, spaces) force quoting the entire `key=value`.
   275→2. **Overloaded operators (`=`, `+=`, `-=`) encoded in a string** — three
   276→   fundamentally different operations (replace, append, remove) masquerade as
   277→   modes of one command via string parsing.
   278→
   279→### Design: Orthogonal verbs with separate args
   280→
   281→Split into four commands on two axes:
   282→
   283→| Axis | Command | Purpose |
   284→|---|---|---|
   285→| Field-level | `ts set` | Set scalar or replace entire array |
   286→| Field-level | `ts unset` | Remove entire field (already exists) |
   287→| Item-level | `ts add` | Add items to array (append or insert) |
   288→| Item-level | `ts remove` | Remove items from array (by value or index) |
   289→
   290→Key and value are separate clap positional arguments. Array values are variadic
   291→(each element is its own shell arg — no brackets, no inner quotes).
   292→
   293→### Syntax
   294→
   295→```bash
   296→# === Field-level (set/unset) ===
   297→
   298→# Scalars — just two words
   299→tspec ts set rustc.lto true
   300→tspec ts set cargo.profile release
   301→tspec ts set panic abort
   302→
   303→# Replace entire array — each element is a separate arg
   304→tspec ts set linker.args -- -static -nostdlib
   305→tspec ts set rustc.build_std core alloc
   306→
   307→# Remove entire field
   308→tspec ts unset linker.args
   309→
   310→# === Item-level (add/remove) ===
   311→
   312→# Append (default add behavior)
   313→tspec ts add linker.args -- -Wl,--gc-sections
   314→tspec ts add rustc.flags -- -Cforce-frame-pointers=yes -Clink-dead-code=no
   315→
   316→# Insert at position
   317→tspec ts add -i 0 linker.args -- -nostdlib
   318→
   319→# Remove by value
   320→tspec ts remove linker.args -- -static
   321→tspec ts remove linker.args -- -static -nostdlib
   322→
   323→# Remove by index
   324→tspec ts remove -i 2 linker.args
   325→```
   326→
   327→### The five array operations
   328→
   329→| # | Operation | Command | Example |
   330→|---|---|---|---|
   331→| 1 | Replace all | `ts set` | `ts set linker.args -- -static -nostdlib` |
   332→| 2 | Clear field | `ts unset` | `ts unset linker.args` |
   333→| 3 | Append items | `ts add` | `ts add linker.args -- -Wl,--gc-sections` |
   334→| 4 | Insert at position | `ts add -i N` | `ts add -i 0 linker.args -- -nostdlib` |
   335→| 5 | Remove by value | `ts remove` | `ts remove linker.args -- -static` |
   336→| 6 | Remove by index | `ts remove -i N` | `ts remove -i 2 linker.args` |
   337→
   338→### Clap definitions
   339→
   340→```rust
   341→/// Set a field (scalar value or replace entire array)
   342→Set {
   343→    /// Field key (e.g., "rustc.lto", "linker.args")
   344→    key: String,
   345→    /// Value(s). For scalars, one value. For arrays, each arg is an element.
   346→    #[arg(required = true)]
   347→    value: Vec<String>,
   348→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
   349→    package: Option<String>,
   350→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
   351→    tspec: Option<String>,
   352→}
   353→
   354→/// Add items to an array field (append by default, or insert at position)
   355→Add {
   356→    /// Field key (must be an array field)
   357→    key: String,
   358→    /// Items to add
   359→    #[arg(required = true)]
   360→    value: Vec<String>,
   361→    /// Insert at this index instead of appending
   362→    #[arg(short = 'i', long = "index")]
   363→    index: Option<usize>,
   364→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
   365→    package: Option<String>,
   366→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
   367→    tspec: Option<String>,
   368→}
   369→
   370→/// Remove items from an array field (by value or by index)
   371→Remove {
   372→    /// Field key (must be an array field)
   373→    key: String,
   374→    /// Items to remove by value (not used with --index)
   375→    value: Vec<String>,
   376→    /// Remove item at this index instead of by value
   377→    #[arg(short = 'i', long = "index")]
   378→    index: Option<usize>,
   379→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
   380→    package: Option<String>,
   381→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
   382→    tspec: Option<String>,
   383→}
   384→```
   385→
   386→### Validation rules
   387→
   388→- `ts set` on a scalar field: exactly one value required
   389→- `ts set` on an array field: one or more values (replaces entire array)
   390→- `ts add`: key must be an array field (error on scalar)
   391→- `ts add -i N`: index must be ≤ current array length
   392→- `ts remove` without `--index`: at least one value required
   393→- `ts remove -i N` with `--index`: no values expected (index is the selector)
   394→- `ts remove -i N`: index must be < current array length
   395→

   415→  Scalars: `values[0]`. Arrays: builds array from all values. Replaces

The fix is `#[arg(allow_hyphen_values = true)]` on the\n`value` field — clap then treats unknown hyphen-prefixed tokens as positional\nvalues. This means **no `--` needed** in normal use:\n\n```bash\ntspec ts set linker.args -static -nostdlib      # just works\ntspec ts add linker.args -Wl,--gc-sections      # just works\n```\n\nThe only edge case is a value that exactly matches a defined short flag (`-p`,\n`-t`, `-i`). In practice no real linker/rustc flag is just `-p` or `-t`, but\n`--` remains available as an escape hatch for that theoretical collision.\n\n### Syntax\n\n```bash\n# === Field-level (set/unset) ===\n\n# Scalars — just two words\ntspec ts set rustc.lto true\ntspec ts set cargo.profile release\ntspec ts set panic abort\n\n# Replace entire array — each element is a separate arg\ntspec ts set linker.args -static -nostdlib\ntspec ts set rustc.build_std core alloc\n\n# Remove entire field\ntspec ts unset linker.args\n\n# === Item-level (add/remove) ===\n\n# Append (default add behavior)\ntspec ts add linker.args -Wl,--gc-sections\ntspec ts add rustc.flags -Cforce-frame-pointers=yes -Clink-dead-code=no\n\n# Insert at position\ntspec ts add -i 0 linker.args -nostdlib\n\n# Remove by value\ntspec ts remove linker.args -static\ntspec ts remove linker.args -static -nostdlib\n\n# Remove by index\ntspec ts remove -i 2 linker.args\n```\n\n### The six array operations\n\n| # | Operation | Command | Example |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | Replace all | `ts set` | `ts set linker.args -static -nostdlib` |\n| 2 | Clear field | `ts unset` | `ts unset linker.args` |\n| 3 | Append items | `ts add` | `ts add linker.args -Wl,--gc-sections` |\n| 4 | Insert at position | `ts add -i N` | `ts add -i 0 linker.args -nostdlib` |\n| 5 | Remove by value | `ts remove` | `ts remove linker.args -static` |\n| 6 | Remove by index | `ts remove -i N` | `ts remove -i 2 linker.args` |"
```json
}
```
