id: toolu_01QxHbNdbTXFmGz8bNpuCJhw
```
    96→## tspec Management (ts subcommand)
    97→
    98→Manage spec files without manual TOML editing. All commands that modify files preserve comments and formatting.
    99→
   100→| Command | Description |
   101→|---------|-------------|
   102→| `ts list` | List tspec files found in the workspace or a specific package |
   103→| `ts show` | Display the TOML contents of a tspec file |
   104→| `ts hash` | Print the content hash (used in backup filenames) of a tspec |
   105→| `ts new` | Create a new tspec with defaults, or copy an existing one with `-f` (byte-for-byte) |
   106→| `ts set` | Set a scalar or replace an entire array (`=`), append to an array (`+=`), or remove from an array (`-=`) |
   107→| `ts unset` | Remove a field (scalar or array) from a tspec entirely |
   108→| `ts backup` | Create a versioned backup copy (`name-NNN-hash.ts.toml`) |
   109→| `ts restore` | Restore a tspec by copying a versioned backup back to its original name |
   110→
   111→```bash
   112→# List / show / hash
   113→tspec ts list                            # List all tspec files
   114→tspec ts list -p myapp                   # List tspec files for a package
   115→tspec ts show -p myapp                   # Show all tspec contents
   116→tspec ts show -p myapp -t tspec-opt      # Show specific tspec-opt.ts.toml
   117→tspec ts hash -p myapp                   # Show content hash of tspec.ts.toml
   118→
   119→# Create new tspec files
   120→tspec ts new -p myapp                    # Create tspec.ts.toml with defaults
   121→tspec ts new experiment -p myapp         # Create experiment.ts.toml
   122→tspec ts new opt2 -p myapp -f tspec-opt  # Copy from existing spec (byte-for-byte)
   123→
   124→# Set values (= sets/replaces, += appends to array, -= removes from array)
   125→tspec ts set strip=symbols -p myapp                # Set a scalar field
   126→tspec ts set rustc.lto=true -t tspec-opt           # Set in a specific tspec
   127→tspec ts set 'linker.args=["-static","-nostdlib"]' # Replace entire array with new value
   128→tspec ts set 'linker.args+=-Wl,--gc-sections'      # Append one item to array
   129→tspec ts set 'linker.args-=-nostdlib'               # Remove one item from array
   130→
   131→# Remove a field entirely
   132→tspec ts unset rustc.lto -p myapp             # Remove scalar field
   133→tspec ts unset linker.args -t tspec-opt       # Remove array field
   134→
   135→# Backup and restore (byte-for-byte copies)
   136→tspec ts backup -p myapp                 # Backup tspec.ts.toml → tspec-001-abcd1234.ts.toml
   137→tspec ts backup -p myapp -t tspec-opt    # Backup tspec-opt.ts.toml
   138→tspec ts restore -t t1-001-abcd1234      # Restore t1.ts.toml from backup
   139→```
   140→
   141→Backups are valid spec files and can be used directly with `-t`.
   142→
   143→`ts set` and `ts unset` use `toml_edit` for surgical editing — comments and formatting are preserved.
   144→`ts backup`, `ts restore`, and `ts new -f` use byte-for-byte file copy — comments are preserved exactly.
   145→
   146→### Supported `ts set` / `ts unset` keys
   147→
   148→| Key | Type | Values |
   149→|-----|------|--------|
   150→| `panic` | scalar | `unwind`, `abort`, `immediate-abort` |
   151→| `strip` | scalar | `none`, `debuginfo`, `symbols` |
   152→| `cargo.profile` | scalar | `debug`, `release` |
   153→| `cargo.target_triple` | scalar | any string |
   154→| `cargo.target_json` | scalar | any path |
   155→| `cargo.target_dir` | scalar | any string (supports `{name}`, `{hash}`) |
   156→| `cargo.unstable` | array | `["flag1", "flag2"]` |
   157→| `rustc.opt_level` | scalar | `0`, `1`, `2`, `3`, `s`, `z` |
   158→| `rustc.panic` | scalar | `abort`, `unwind`, `immediate-abort` |
   159→| `rustc.lto` | scalar | `true`, `false` |
   160→| `rustc.codegen_units` | scalar | integer |
   161→| `rustc.build_std` | array | `["core", "alloc"]` |
   162→| `rustc.flags` | array | `["-C", "flag"]` |
   163→| `linker.args` | array | `["-static", "-nostdlib"]` |
   164→
   165→### Array value rules
   166→
   167→Array values follow two simple rules, consistent across `=`, `+=`, and `-=`:
   168→
   169→- **With brackets** `["a","b"]` — TOML inline array. Commas separate items. Quotes protect literal commas.
   170→- **Without brackets** `value` — always **one item**. The entire value is taken literally.
   171→
   172→| Syntax | Result |
   173→|--------|--------|
   174→| `["-static","-nostdlib"]` | 2 items: `-static`, `-nostdlib` |
   175→| `["-Wl,--gc-sections"]` | 1 item: `-Wl,--gc-sections` (comma inside quotes is literal) |
   176→| `-static` | 1 item: `-static` |
   177→| `-Wl,--gc-sections` | 1 item: `-Wl,--gc-sections` |
   178→| `core,alloc` | 1 item: `core,alloc` (NOT two items — use `["core","alloc"]`) |
   179→
   180→```bash
   181→# Replace entire array with multiple items (bracket syntax, quote for shell)
   182→tspec ts set 'linker.args=["-static","-nostdlib"]'
   183→tspec ts set 'rustc.build_std=["core","alloc"]'
   184→
   185→# Replace entire array with a single item (no brackets needed)
   186→tspec ts set linker.args=-static                   # array becomes ["-static"]
   187→
   188→# Append to an existing array
   189→tspec ts set 'linker.args+=-nostdlib'              # append one item
   190→tspec ts set 'linker.args+=["-nostdlib","-pie"]'   # append multiple items
   191→
   192→# Remove from an array
   193→tspec ts set 'linker.args-=-nostdlib'              # remove one item
   194→tspec ts set 'linker.args-=["-static","-pie"]'     # remove multiple items
   195→
   196→# += skips duplicates; -= is a no-op if the value isn't present.
   197→# If -= removes the last entry, the field is removed entirely.
   198→
   199→# To remove the entire field, use unset:
   200→tspec ts unset linker.args                         # removes the field completely
   201→```
   202→
   203→Note: Use `ts` as the subcommand (short for "tspec management").
   204→
   205→## Testing
```

Remove array field\n\n# Backup and restore (byte-for-byte copies)\ntspec ts backup -p myapp                 # Backup tspec.ts.toml → tspec-001-abcd1234.ts.toml\ntspec ts backup -p myapp -t tspec-opt    # Backup tspec-opt.ts.toml\ntspec ts restore -t t1-001-abcd1234      # Restore t1.ts.toml from backup\n```\n\nBackups are valid spec files and can be used directly with `-t`.\n\n`ts set` and `ts unset` use `toml_edit` for surgical editing — comments and formatting are preserved.\n`ts backup`, `ts restore`, and `ts new -f` use byte-for-byte file copy — comments are preserved exactly.\n\n### Supported `ts set` / `ts unset` keys\n\n| Key | Type | Values |\n|-----|------|--------|\n| `panic` | scalar | `unwind`, `abort`, `immediate-abort` |\n| `strip` | scalar | `none`, `debuginfo`, `symbols` |\n| `cargo.profile` | scalar | `debug`, `release` |\n| `cargo.target_triple` | scalar | any string |\n| `cargo.target_json` | scalar | any path |\n| `cargo.target_dir` | scalar | any string (supports `{name}`, `{hash}`) |\n| `cargo.unstable` | array | `[\"flag1\", \"flag2\"]` |\n| `rustc.opt_level` | scalar | `0`, `1`, `2`, `3`, `s`, `z` |\n| `rustc.panic` | scalar | `abort`, `unwind`, `immediate-abort` |\n| `rustc.lto` | scalar | `true`, `false` |\n| `rustc.codegen_units` | scalar | integer |\n| `rustc.build_std` | array | `[\"core\", \"alloc\"]` |\n| `rustc.flags` | array | `[\"-C\", \"flag\"]` |\n| `linker.args` | array | `[\"-static\", \"-nostdlib\"]` |\n\n### Array value rules\n\nArray values follow two simple rules, consistent across `=`, `+=`, and `-=`:\n\n- **With brackets** `[\"a\",\"b\"]` — TOML inline array. Commas separate items. Quotes protect literal commas.\n- **Without brackets** `value` — always **one item**. The entire value is taken literally.\n\n| Syntax | Result |\n|--------|--------|\n| `[\"-static\",\"-nostdlib\"]` | 2 items: `-static`, `-nostdlib` |\n| `[\"-Wl,--gc-sections\"]` | 1 item: `-Wl,--gc-sections` (comma inside quotes is literal) |\n| `-static` | 1 item: `-static` |\n| `-Wl,--gc-sections` | 1 item: `-Wl,--gc-sections` |\n| `core,alloc` | 1 item: `core,alloc` (NOT two items — use `[\"core\",\"alloc\"]`) |\n\n```bash\n# Replace entire array with multiple items (bracket syntax, quote for shell)\ntspec ts set 'linker.args=[\"-static\",\"-nostdlib\"]'\ntspec ts set 'rustc.build_std=[\"core\",\"alloc\"]'\n\n# Replace entire array with a single item (no brackets needed)\ntspec ts set linker.args=-static                   # array becomes [\"-static\"]\n\n# Append to an existing array\ntspec ts set 'linker.args+=-nostdlib'              # append one item\ntspec ts set 'linker.args+=[\"-nostdlib\",\"-pie\"]'   # append multiple items\n\n# Remove from an array\ntspec ts set 'linker.args-=-nostdlib'              # remove one item\ntspec ts set 'linker.args-=[\"-static\",\"-pie\"]'     # remove multiple items\n\n# += skips duplicates; -= is a no-op if the value isn't present.\n# If -= removes the last entry, the field is removed entirely.\n\n# To remove the entire field, use unset:\ntspec ts unset linker.args                         # removes the field completely\n```\n\nNote: Use `ts` as the subcommand (short for \"tspec management\").",
  "new_string": "| Command | Description |\n|---------|-------------|\n| `ts list` | List tspec files found in the workspace or a specific package |\n| `ts show` | Display the TOML contents of a tspec file |\n| `ts hash` | Print the content hash (used in backup filenames) of a tspec |\n| `ts new` | Create a new tspec with defaults, or copy an existing one with `-f` (byte-for-byte) |\n| `ts set` | Set a scalar value or replace an entire array |\n| `ts unset` | Remove a field (scalar or array) from a tspec entirely |\n| `ts add` | Add items to an array field (append or insert at position) |\n| `ts remove` | Remove items from an array field (by value or by index) |\n| `ts backup` | Create a versioned backup copy (`name-NNN-hash.ts.toml`) |\n| `ts restore` | Restore a tspec by copying a versioned backup back to its original name |\n\n```bash\n# List / show / hash\ntspec ts list                            # List all tspec files\ntspec ts list -p myapp                   # List tspec files for a package\ntspec ts show -p myapp                   # Show all tspec contents\ntspec ts show -p myapp -t tspec-opt      # Show specific tspec-opt.ts.toml\ntspec ts hash -p myapp                   # Show content hash of tspec.ts.toml\n\n# Create new tspec files\ntspec ts new -p myapp                    # Create tspec.ts.toml with defaults\ntspec ts new experiment -p myapp         # Create experiment.ts.toml\ntspec ts new opt2 -p myapp -f tspec-opt  # Copy from existing spec (byte-for-byte)\n\n# Set scalar values\ntspec ts set strip symbols -p myapp              # Set a scalar field\ntspec ts set rustc.lto true -t tspec-opt         # Set in a specific tspec\ntspec ts set cargo.profile release               # No quoting needed\n\n# Set (replace) entire arrays — each element is a separate arg\ntspec ts set linker.args -static -nostdlib        # Replace array\ntspec ts set rustc.build_std core alloc           # Replace array\n\n# Add items to arrays (append by default)\ntspec ts add linker.args -Wl,--gc-sections        # Append one item\ntspec ts add linker.args -nostdlib -pie            # Append multiple items\ntspec ts add -i 0 linker.args -nostartfiles        # Insert at position 0\n\n# Remove items from arrays\ntspec ts remove linker.args -nostdlib              # Remove by value\ntspec ts remove linker.args -static -pie           # Remove multiple by value\ntspec ts remove -i 2 linker.args                   # Remove by index\n\n# Remove a field entirely\ntspec ts unset rustc.lto -p myapp                  # Remove scalar field\ntspec ts unset linker.args -t tspec-opt            # Remove array field\n\n# Backup and restore (byte-for-byte copies)\ntspec ts backup -p myapp                 # Backup tspec.ts.toml → tspec-001-abcd1234.ts.toml\ntspec ts backup -p myapp -t tspec-opt    # Backup tspec-opt.ts.toml\ntspec ts restore -t t1-001-abcd1234      # Restore t1.ts.toml from backup\n```\n\nBackups are valid spec files and can be used directly with `-t`.\n\n`ts set`, `ts unset`, `ts add`, and `ts remove` use `toml_edit` for surgical editing — comments and formatting are preserved.\n`ts backup`, `ts restore`, and `ts new -f` use byte-for-byte file copy — comments are preserved exactly.\n\n### Supported keys\n\n| Key | Type | Values |\n|-----|------|--------|\n| `panic` | scalar | `unwind`, `abort`, `immediate-abort` |\n| `strip` | scalar | `none`, `debuginfo`, `symbols` |\n| `cargo.profile` | scalar | `debug`, `release` |\n| `cargo.target_triple` | scalar | any string |\n| `cargo.target_json` | scalar | any path |\n| `cargo.target_dir` | scalar | any string (supports `{name}`, `{hash}`) |\n| `cargo.unstable` | array | e.g. `panic-immediate-abort` |\n| `rustc.opt_level` | scalar | `0`, `1`, `2`, `3`, `s`, `z` |\n| `rustc.panic` | scalar | `abort`, `unwind`, `immediate-abort` |\n| `rustc.lto` | scalar | `true`, `false` |\n| `rustc.codegen_units` | scalar | integer |\n| `rustc.build_std` | array | e.g. `core alloc` |\n| `rustc.flags` | array | e.g. `-Cforce-frame-pointers=yes` |\n| `linker.args` | array | e.g. `-static -nostdlib` |\n\n### Array operations\n\nFour orthogonal commands operate on arrays at two levels:\n\n| Level | Command | Purpose |\n|-------|---------|---------|\n| Field-level | `ts set` | Replace entire array with new values |\n| Field-level | `ts unset` | Remove the array field entirely |\n| Item-level | `ts add` | Append items (or insert at position with `-i`) |\n| Item-level | `ts remove` | Remove items by value (or by index with `-i`) |\n\n```bash\ntspec ts set linker.args -static -nostdlib   # replace all with these\ntspec ts unset linker.args                   # clear the field\ntspec ts add linker.args -Wl,--gc-sections   # append (skips duplicates)\ntspec ts add -i 0 linker.args -nostartfiles  # insert at position\ntspec ts remove linker.args -static          # remove by value\ntspec ts remove -i 2 linker.args             # remove by index\n```\n\nValues starting with `-` work without quoting thanks to `allow_hyphen_values`.\nUse `--` as an escape hatch if a value collides with a flag like `-p`, `-t`, or `-i`.\n\nNote: Use `ts` as the subcommand (short for \"tspec management\")."
}

Stopping. Here's what I found — out-of-date comments that need updating:

**In source code (`src/ts_cmd/edit.rs`):**
- Line 251: `remove_items_by_value` doc says "If the array becomes empty, removes the field (and empty table)."
- Line 274: `remove_item_by_index` doc says same
- Line 299: `unset_field` doc says "If the containing table becomes empty, remove it too." — this one is fine for `ts unset`, but wrong for `ts remove`

**In chores-3.md plan:**
- Line 425-426: "If array becomes empty, removes the field (existing behavior)."
- Line 429: "the given index. If array becomes empty, removes the field."
- Line 510: test description says "empty-removes-field"

**In README.md** — already updated, but the `ts remove` section doesn't mention empty array behavior

**The fix for `edit.rs`:** Change `remove_items_by_value` and `remove_item_by_index` to leave an empty array `[]` in place (and keep the containing table), instead of calling `unset_field`. The `unset_field` behavior (removing empty tables) stays as-is since `ts unset` is an explicit "delete this field" action.

Want me to proceed with these changes?

> DEVELOPER

When removing an item do not remove the table, also I'm thinking empty fields are OK, thoughts

> AGENT

Agreed — empty fields should stay. The reasoning:

1. **Semantic difference**: `linker.args = []` means "I explicitly have no linker args." A missing `linker.args` means "I haven't specified linker args" (could inherit defaults later). `ts remove` shouldn't collapse one into the other.

2. **Orthogonality**: `ts remove` is item-level — it removes *items*, not *fields*. If you want the field gone, that's `ts unset`. Each command does one thing.

3. **Least surprise**: Removing `-static` from `linker.args` shouldn't make the `[linker]` section header vanish.

So the behavior should be:

| Command | Empty array result | Empty table result |
|---|---|---|
| `ts remove` | Keeps `args = []` | Keeps `[linker]` |
| `ts unset` | Removes the field | Removes empty table (current behavior, still correct) |

The change is small — in `remove_items_by_value` and `remove_item_by_index`, replace the `unset_field` call with `set_array_in_doc(doc, key, new_arr)` when the array is empty. Want me to go ahead?

> DEVELOPER

I'm thinking we shouldn't ever remove empty tables, the user can do that if they want?

> AGENT

Fair point. `ts unset` removes a *field*, not a *table*. If you unset `linker.args` and `[linker]` becomes empty, the section header staying is predictable — the command did exactly what you asked, nothing more.

If empty sections bother you, clean them up manually or add a `ts clean` command later. But auto-removing tables is a side effect the user didn't ask for.

Let me fix all three: `remove_items_by_value`, `remove_item_by_index` (keep empty arrays, keep tables), and `unset_field` (keep empty tables).

   250→/// Remove items from an array field by value.
   251→/// If the array becomes empty, removes the field (and empty table).
   252→pub fn remove_items_by_value(doc: &mut DocumentMut, key: &str, values: &[String]) -> Result<()> {
   253→    let arr = get_existing_array(doc, key);
   254→
   255→    let mut new_arr = Array::new();
   256→    for item in arr.iter() {
   257→        if let Some(s) = item.as_str()
   258→            && !values.iter().any(|v| v == s)
   259→        {
   260→            new_arr.push(s);
   261→        }
   262→    }
   263→
   264→    if new_arr.is_empty() {
   265→        unset_field(doc, key)?;
   266→    } else {
   267→        set_array_in_doc(doc, key, new_arr);
   268→    }
   269→
   270→    Ok(())
   271→}
   272→
   273→/// Remove an item from an array field by index.
   274→/// If the array becomes empty, removes the field (and empty table).
   275→pub fn remove_item_by_index(doc: &mut DocumentMut, key: &str, index: usize) -> Result<()> {
   276→    let mut arr = get_existing_array(doc, key);
   277→
   278→    if index >= arr.len() {
   279→        bail!(
   280→            "index {} out of bounds for array '{}' with {} elements",
   281→            index,
   282→            key,
   283→            arr.len()
   284→        );
   285→    }
   286→
   287→    arr.remove(index);
   288→
   289→    if arr.is_empty() {
   290→        unset_field(doc, key)?;
   291→    } else {
   292→        set_array_in_doc(doc, key, arr);
   293→    }
   294→
   295→    Ok(())
   296→}
   297→
   298→/// Remove a field from a toml_edit document.
   299→/// If the containing table becomes empty, remove it too.
   300→pub fn unset_field(doc: &mut DocumentMut, key: &str) -> Result<()> {
   301→    let (table_name, field) = parse_key(key);
   302→
   303→    match table_name {
   304→        Some(table) => {
   305→            if let Some(Item::Table(tbl)) = doc.get_mut(table) {
   306→                tbl.remove(field);
   307→                // If table is now empty, remove it
   308→                if tbl.is_empty() {
   309→                    doc.remove(table);
   310→                }
   311→            }
   312→        }
   313→        None => {
   314→            doc.remove(field);
   315→        }
   316→    }
   317→
   318→    Ok(())
   319→}

  "old_string": "/// Remove items from an array field by value.\n/// If the array becomes empty, removes the field (and empty table).\npub fn remove_items_by_value(doc: &mut DocumentMut, key: &str, values: &[String]) -> Result<()> {\n    let arr = get_existing_array(doc, key);\n\n    let mut new_arr = Array::new();\n    for item in arr.iter() {\n        if let Some(s) = item.as_str()\n            && !values.iter().any(|v| v == s)\n        {\n            new_arr.push(s);\n        }\n    }\n\n    if new_arr.is_empty() {\n        unset_field(doc, key)?;\n    } else {\n        set_array_in_doc(doc, key, new_arr);\n    }\n\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Remove an item from an array field by index.\n/// If the array becomes empty, removes the field (and empty table).\npub fn remove_item_by_index(doc: &mut DocumentMut, key: &str, index: usize) -> Result<()> {\n    let mut arr = get_existing_array(doc, key);\n\n    if index >= arr.len() {\n        bail!(\n            \"index {} out of bounds for array '{}' with {} elements\",\n            index,\n            key,\n            arr.len()\n        );\n    }\n\n    arr.remove(index);\n\n    if arr.is_empty() {\n        unset_field(doc, key)?;\n    } else {\n        set_array_in_doc(doc, key, arr);\n    }\n\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Remove a field from a toml_edit document.\n/// If the containing table becomes empty, remove it too.\npub fn unset_field(doc: &mut DocumentMut, key: &str) -> Result<()> {\n    let (table_name, field) = parse_key(key);\n\n    match table_name {\n        Some(table) => {\n            if let Some(Item::Table(tbl)) = doc.get_mut(table) {\n                tbl.remove(field);\n                // If table is now empty, remove it\n                if tbl.is_empty() {\n                    doc.remove(table);\n                }\n            }\n        }\n        None => {\n            doc.remove(field);\n        }\n    }\n\n    Ok(())\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Remove items from an array field by value.\n/// Keeps the field as an empty array if all items are removed.\npub fn remove_items_by_value(doc: &mut DocumentMut, key: &str, values: &[String]) -> Result<()> {\n    let arr = get_existing_array(doc, key);\n\n    let mut new_arr = Array::new();\n    for item in arr.iter() {\n        if let Some(s) = item.as_str()\n            && !values.iter().any(|v| v == s)\n        {\n            new_arr.push(s);\n        }\n    }\n\n    set_array_in_doc(doc, key, new_arr);\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Remove an item from an array field by index.\n/// Keeps the field as an empty array if the last item is removed.\npub fn remove_item_by_index(doc: &mut DocumentMut, key: &str, index: usize) -> Result<()> {\n    let mut arr = get_existing_array(doc, key);\n\n    if index >= arr.len() {\n        bail!(\n            \"index {} out of bounds for array '{}' with {} elements\",\n            index,\n            key,\n            arr.len()\n        );\n    }\n\n    arr.remove(index);\n    set_array_in_doc(doc, key, arr);\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Remove a field from a toml_edit document.\n/// Does not remove the containing table, even if it becomes empty.\npub fn unset_field(doc: &mut DocumentMut, key: &str) -> Result<()> {\n    let (table_name, field) = parse_key(key);\n\n    match table_name {\n        Some(table) => {\n            if let Some(Item::Table(tbl)) = doc.get_mut(table) {\n                tbl.remove(field);\n            }\n        }\n        None => {\n            doc.remove(field);\n        }\n    }\n\n    Ok(())\n}"
}

> AGENT

The `remove.rs` tests at lines 131 and 147 still assert `is_empty()` which is correct — the serde-loaded `Vec<String>` will be empty for `args = []`. Those tests are fine. The `unset.rs` test is also fine — unsetting the field means it won't be in the loaded spec. Let me just rename the `remove.rs` test functions to reflect the new semantics.

     1→//! toml_edit helpers for surgical tspec editing that preserves comments/formatting.
     2→
     3→use anyhow::{Result, bail};
     4→use toml_edit::{Array, DocumentMut, Item, Value};
     5→
     6→/// Whether a field holds a scalar or an array.
     7→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
     8→pub enum FieldKind {
     9→    Scalar,
    10→    Array,
    11→}
    12→
    13→/// Registry entry: (dotted key path, kind).
    14→const FIELD_REGISTRY: &[(&str, FieldKind)] = &[
    15→    ("panic", FieldKind::Scalar),
    16→    ("strip", FieldKind::Scalar),
    17→    ("cargo.profile", FieldKind::Scalar),
    18→    ("cargo.target_triple", FieldKind::Scalar),
    19→    ("cargo.target_json", FieldKind::Scalar),
    20→    ("cargo.target_dir", FieldKind::Scalar),
    21→    ("cargo.unstable", FieldKind::Array),
    22→    ("rustc.opt_level", FieldKind::Scalar),
    23→    ("rustc.panic", FieldKind::Scalar),
    24→    ("rustc.lto", FieldKind::Scalar),
    25→    ("rustc.codegen_units", FieldKind::Scalar),
    26→    ("rustc.build_std", FieldKind::Array),
    27→    ("rustc.flags", FieldKind::Array),
    28→    ("linker.args", FieldKind::Array),
    29→];
    30→
    31→/// Validate that a key is in the registry and return its kind.
    32→pub fn validate_key(key: &str) -> Result<FieldKind> {
    33→    for &(k, kind) in FIELD_REGISTRY {
    34→        if k == key {
    35→            return Ok(kind);
    36→        }
    37→    }
    38→    let valid_keys: Vec<&str> = FIELD_REGISTRY.iter().map(|(k, _)| *k).collect();
    39→    bail!(
    40→        "unknown key: {} (valid keys: {})",
    41→        key,
    42→        valid_keys.join(", ")
    43→    )
    44→}
    45→
    46→/// Validate a value for enum-constrained fields.
    47→/// For unconstrained fields (strings, arrays), accepts anything.
    48→pub fn validate_value(key: &str, value: &str) -> Result<()> {
    49→    match key {
    50→        "panic" => match value {
    51→            "unwind" | "abort" | "immediate-abort" => Ok(()),
    52→            _ => bail!(
    53→                "invalid panic mode: {} (expected: unwind, abort, immediate-abort)",
    54→                value
    55→            ),
    56→        },
    57→        "strip" => match value {
    58→            "none" | "debuginfo" | "symbols" => Ok(()),
    59→            _ => bail!(
    60→                "invalid strip mode: {} (expected: none, debuginfo, symbols)",
    61→                value
    62→            ),
    63→        },
    64→        "cargo.profile" => match value {
    65→            "debug" | "release" => Ok(()),
    66→            _ => bail!("invalid profile: {} (expected: debug, release)", value),
    67→        },
    68→        "rustc.opt_level" => match value {
    69→            "0" | "1" | "2" | "3" | "s" | "z" => Ok(()),
    70→            _ => bail!("invalid opt-level: {} (expected: 0, 1, 2, 3, s, z)", value),
    71→        },
    72→        "rustc.panic" => match value {
    73→            "abort" | "unwind" | "immediate-abort" => Ok(()),
    74→            _ => bail!(
    75→                "invalid panic strategy: {} (expected: abort, unwind, immediate-abort)",
    76→                value
    77→            ),
    78→        },
    79→        "rustc.lto" => match value {
    80→            "true" | "false" | "yes" | "no" | "1" | "0" => Ok(()),
    81→            _ => bail!("invalid boolean: {} (expected: true/false)", value),
    82→        },
    83→        "rustc.codegen_units" => {
    84→            value.parse::<u32>().map_err(|_| {
    85→                anyhow::anyhow!("invalid codegen_units: {} (expected integer)", value)
    86→            })?;
    87→            Ok(())
    88→        }
    89→        _ => Ok(()),
    90→    }
    91→}
    92→
    93→/// Parse a key into (optional table, field).
    94→/// "panic" -> (None, "panic")
    95→/// "rustc.lto" -> (Some("rustc"), "lto")
    96→fn parse_key(key: &str) -> (Option<&str>, &str) {
    97→    match key.split_once('.') {
    98→        Some((table, field)) => (Some(table), field),
    99→        None => (None, key),
   100→    }
   101→}
   102→
   103→/// Parse a value string into a toml_edit Value.
   104→/// Booleans -> bool, integers -> i64, everything else -> string.
   105→fn parse_scalar_value(key: &str, raw: &str) -> Value {
   106→    // For rustc.lto, always parse as boolean
   107→    if key == "rustc.lto" {
   108→        return match raw {
   109→            "true" | "yes" | "1" => Value::from(true),
   110→            _ => Value::from(false),
   111→        };
   112→    }
   113→
   114→    // For rustc.codegen_units, always parse as integer
   115→    if key == "rustc.codegen_units"
   116→        && let Ok(n) = raw.parse::<i64>()
   117→    {
   118→        return Value::from(n);
   119→    }
   120→
   121→    // For rustc.opt_level, keep as string (since "0","1",etc. are enum variants)
   122→    Value::from(raw)
   123→}
   124→
   125→/// Get the existing array for a field, or an empty array if it doesn't exist.
   126→fn get_existing_array(doc: &DocumentMut, key: &str) -> Array {
   127→    let (table_name, field) = parse_key(key);
   128→    match table_name {
   129→        Some(table) => doc
   130→            .get(table)
   131→            .and_then(|t| t.get(field))
   132→            .and_then(|v| v.as_array())
   133→            .cloned()
   134→            .unwrap_or_default(),
   135→        None => doc
   136→            .get(field)
   137→            .and_then(|v| v.as_array())
   138→            .cloned()
   139→            .unwrap_or_default(),
   140→    }
   141→}
   142→
   143→/// Set an array value into the document at the given key path.
   144→fn set_array_in_doc(doc: &mut DocumentMut, key: &str, arr: Array) {
   145→    let (table_name, field) = parse_key(key);
   146→    match table_name {
   147→        Some(table) => {
   148→            ensure_table(doc, table);
   149→            doc[table][field] = Item::Value(Value::Array(arr));
   150→        }
   151→        None => {
   152→            doc[field] = Item::Value(Value::Array(arr));
   153→        }
   154→    }
   155→}
   156→
   157→/// Ensure a table exists in the document.
   158→fn ensure_table(doc: &mut DocumentMut, table: &str) {
   159→    if doc.get(table).is_none() {
   160→        doc[table] = Item::Table(toml_edit::Table::new());
   161→    }
   162→}
   163→
   164→// === Public API: takes &[String] from shell args, no string parsing ===
   165→
   166→/// Set a field from string args. Scalars take `values[0]`, arrays take all values.
   167→pub fn set_field(
   168→    doc: &mut DocumentMut,
   169→    key: &str,
   170→    values: &[String],
   171→    kind: FieldKind,
   172→) -> Result<()> {
   173→    let (table_name, field) = parse_key(key);
   174→
   175→    match kind {
   176→        FieldKind::Scalar => {
   177→            if values.len() != 1 {
   178→                bail!(
   179→                    "scalar field '{}' requires exactly one value, got {}",
   180→                    key,
   181→                    values.len()
   182→                );
   183→            }
   184→            let val = parse_scalar_value(key, &values[0]);
   185→            match table_name {
   186→                Some(table) => {
   187→                    ensure_table(doc, table);
   188→                    doc[table][field] = Item::Value(val);
   189→                }
   190→                None => {
   191→                    doc[field] = Item::Value(val);
   192→                }
   193→            }
   194→        }
   195→        FieldKind::Array => {
   196→            let mut arr = Array::new();
   197→            for v in values {
   198→                arr.push(v.as_str());
   199→            }
   200→            set_array_in_doc(doc, key, arr);
   201→        }
   202→    }
   203→
   204→    Ok(())
   205→}
   206→
   207→/// Add items to an array field. Appends by default, or inserts at `index`.
   208→/// Deduplicates on append; insert adds at position without dedup.
   209→pub fn add_items(
   210→    doc: &mut DocumentMut,
   211→    key: &str,
   212→    values: &[String],
   213→    index: Option<usize>,
   214→) -> Result<()> {
   215→    let mut arr = get_existing_array(doc, key);
   216→
   217→    match index {
   218→        Some(idx) => {
   219→            if idx > arr.len() {
   220→                bail!(
   221→                    "index {} out of bounds for array '{}' with {} elements",
   222→                    idx,
   223→                    key,
   224→                    arr.len()
   225→                );
   226→            }
   227→            // Insert at position (no dedup — user explicitly chose position)
   228→            for (offset, v) in values.iter().enumerate() {
   229→                arr.insert(idx + offset, v.as_str());
   230→            }
   231→        }
   232→        None => {
   233→            // Append with dedup
   234→            let existing: Vec<String> = arr
   235→                .iter()
   236→                .filter_map(|v| v.as_str().map(|s| s.to_string()))
   237→                .collect();
   238→            for v in values {
   239→                if !existing.contains(v) {
   240→                    arr.push(v.as_str());
   241→                }
   242→            }
   243→        }
   244→    }
   245→
   246→    set_array_in_doc(doc, key, arr);
   247→    Ok(())
   248→}
   249→
   250→/// Remove items from an array field by value.
   251→/// Keeps the field as an empty array if all items are removed.
   252→pub fn remove_items_by_value(doc: &mut DocumentMut, key: &str, values: &[String]) -> Result<()> {
   253→    let arr = get_existing_array(doc, key);
   254→
   255→    let mut new_arr = Array::new();
   256→    for item in arr.iter() {
   257→        if let Some(s) = item.as_str()
   258→            && !values.iter().any(|v| v == s)
   259→        {
   260→            new_arr.push(s);
   261→        }
   262→    }
   263→
   264→    set_array_in_doc(doc, key, new_arr);
   265→    Ok(())
   266→}
   267→
   268→/// Remove an item from an array field by index.
   269→/// Keeps the field as an empty array if the last item is removed.
   270→pub fn remove_item_by_index(doc: &mut DocumentMut, key: &str, index: usize) -> Result<()> {
   271→    let mut arr = get_existing_array(doc, key);
   272→
   273→    if index >= arr.len() {
   274→        bail!(
   275→            "index {} out of bounds for array '{}' with {} elements",
   276→            index,
   277→            key,
   278→            arr.len()
   279→        );
   280→    }
   281→
   282→    arr.remove(index);
   283→    set_array_in_doc(doc, key, arr);
   284→    Ok(())
   285→}
   286→
   287→/// Remove a field from a toml_edit document.
   288→/// Does not remove the containing table, even if it becomes empty.
   289→pub fn unset_field(doc: &mut DocumentMut, key: &str) -> Result<()> {
   290→    let (table_name, field) = parse_key(key);
   291→
   292→    match table_name {
   293→        Some(table) => {
   294→            if let Some(Item::Table(tbl)) = doc.get_mut(table) {
   295→                tbl.remove(field);
   296→            }
   297→        }
   298→        None => {
   299→            doc.remove(field);
   300→        }
   301→    }
   302→
   303→    Ok(())
   304→}

> DEVELOPER

You always make the mistake of not passing `--path .`. Personally I'd lean towards not requiring it, but that's what cargo does and the rationale is probably it's a permanent change unreversable so being specific is reasonable, thoughts?

> AGENT

Good point. Cargo requires `--path .` as a safety measure — installing is a permanent system-level change, so being explicit about the source is reasonable. Same philosophy as requiring `--force` for destructive operations. I'll remember: `tspec install --path .`

     5→## 20260209 - ts set/unset/backup/restore improvements using toml_edit
     6→
     7→### Context
     8→
     9→`ts set` currently only supports ~10 hardcoded scalar keys and silently ignores all array/nested fields (`rustc.build_std`, `rustc.flags`, `linker.args`, `cargo.unstable`, `cargo.target_json`, `linker.version_script`). Users get `Error: unknown key` for documented fields. Additionally, `ts backup`, `ts restore`, and `ts new -f` round-trip through serde serialization, which destroys user comments and formatting.
    10→
    11→### Three write strategies
    12→
    13→| Operation | Strategy | Why |
    14→|---|---|---|
    15→| load, hash, build | serde (`toml`) | Need typed `Spec` struct for build logic |
    16→| set, unset | `toml_edit` (`DocumentMut`) | Surgical edit preserving comments/formatting |
    17→| backup, restore, new -f | raw `fs::copy` | Exact byte-for-byte preservation |
    18→
    19→### Step 1: Add `toml_edit` dependency
    20→
    21→**File:** `Cargo.toml`
    22→
    23→Add `toml_edit = "0.22"` to `[dependencies]`. Already a transitive dep via `toml 0.8`, so no new download.
    24→
    25→### Step 2: Create `src/ts_cmd/edit.rs` - toml_edit helper module
    26→
    27→New module with:
    28→
    29→- **`FIELD_REGISTRY`** - static list of all valid field paths with their types:
    30→  ```
    31→  ("panic", Scalar)           ("strip", Scalar)
    32→  ("cargo.profile", Scalar)   ("cargo.target_triple", Scalar)
    33→  ("cargo.target_json", Scalar) ("cargo.target_dir", Scalar)
    34→  ("cargo.unstable", Array)   ("rustc.opt_level", Scalar)
    35→  ("rustc.panic", Scalar)     ("rustc.lto", Scalar)
    36→  ("rustc.codegen_units", Scalar) ("rustc.build_std", Array)
    37→  ("rustc.flags", Array)      ("linker.args", Array)
    38→  ```
    39→  (Skip `linker.version_script` for now - it's a nested table with `global` array + `local` string, more complex.)
    40→
    41→- **`validate_key(key) -> Result<FieldKind>`** - checks key against registry, returns `Scalar` or `Array`
    42→
    43→- **`set_field(doc, key, value) -> Result<()>`** - for scalars: parse key path -> ensure table exists -> set `doc["section"]["field"] = value`. Value parsing: booleans as `toml_edit::value(true/false)`, integers as `toml_edit::value(i64)`, everything else as string. For arrays: parse `[a,b,c]` or `"a","b"` syntax -> replace the array.
    44→
    45→- **`unset_field(doc, key) -> Result<()>`** - parse key path -> remove from table. If table becomes empty, remove the table too.
    46→
    47→- **`validate_value(key, value) -> Result<()>`** - reuse existing parse functions (`parse_panic_mode`, `parse_strip_mode`, etc.) for enum keys. Accept anything for string/array keys.
    48→
    49→### Step 3: Rewrite `src/ts_cmd/set.rs` to use toml_edit
    50→
    51→Replace serde round-trip with:
    52→1. Read file as raw string (or empty string if file doesn't exist)
    53→2. Parse into `DocumentMut`
    54→3. Validate key via `edit::validate_key()`
    55→4. Validate value via `edit::validate_value()` (for enum fields)
    56→5. Call `edit::set_field(doc, key, value)`
    57→6. Write `doc.to_string()` back to file
    58→
    59→Remove `apply_value()` and all parse helper functions from set.rs (move validation to edit.rs).
    60→
    61→Update tests to test the new toml_edit-based flow. Add tests for previously-missing keys (`rustc.build_std`, `linker.args`, `cargo.unstable`, etc.).
    62→

    64→
    65→New file:
    66→1. Resolve package dir and tspec path (same pattern as set.rs)
    67→2. Read file as raw string
    68→3. Parse into `DocumentMut`
    69→4. Validate key via `edit::validate_key()`

   164→
   165→## 20260210 - Shell-hostile characters in values and array syntax
   166→
   167→### Problem
   168→
   169→Several tspec features used characters that conflict with bash:
   170→
   171→1. **`<name>`/`<hash>` placeholders in `cargo.target_dir`** — `<` and `>` are shell
   172→   redirection operators, so `tspec ts set cargo.target_dir=<name>-xyz` triggers
   173→   `bash: name: No such file or directory`. Users must quote the whole argument.
   174→
   175→2. **Bracket array syntax with unquoted values** — `tspec ts set linker.args-=[za]`
   176→   fails because `parse_array_value` tries to parse `[za]` as a TOML inline array,
   177→   and bare `za` is not valid TOML (must be `["za"]`). Meanwhile `linker.args+=za`
   178→   (no brackets) works fine since the bare path treats it as a single string.
   179→
   180→3. **Shell quote stripping compounds the bracket problem** — even
   181→   `tspec ts set linker.args-=["za"]` fails because bash strips the double quotes,
   182→   passing `[za]` to tspec. Only single-quoting the entire argument works:
   183→   `tspec ts set 'linker.args-=["za"]'`.
   184→
   185→### Fix 1: `<>` → `{}` placeholders (v0.9.8)
   186→
   187→Replaced `<name>`/`<hash>` with `{name}`/`{hash}` in `expand_target_dir()`. Curly
   188→braces are shell-inert — bash brace expansion only triggers with commas (`{a,b}`) or
   189→ranges (`{1..3}`) inside, so `{name}` passes through untouched.
   190→
   191→**Files changed:** `src/tspec.rs`, `src/types.rs`, `src/ts_cmd/set.rs` (test),
   192→`README.md`
   193→
   194→### Fix 2: Bare-string fallback for bracket array syntax (v0.9.8)
   195→
   196→Changed `parse_array_value()` to try TOML parsing first, then fall back to
   197→comma-splitting bare strings when TOML fails. This means:
   198→
   199→| Input | Parse path | Result |
   200→|---|---|---|
   201→| `za` | bare (no brackets) | single item `"za"` |
   202→| `[za]` | TOML fails → bare fallback | single item `"za"` |
   203→| `[-static,-nostdlib]` | TOML fails → bare fallback | two items |
   204→| `[-static, -nostdlib]` | TOML fails → bare fallback | two items (spaces trimmed) |
   205→| `["-Wl,--gc-sections", "-static"]` | TOML succeeds | two items (embedded comma preserved) |
   206→
   207→The TOML-first path is preserved so that properly quoted strings with embedded commas
   208→still work — `"-Wl,--gc-sections"` contains a comma that must not be split on.
   209→
   210→**Files changed:** `src/ts_cmd/edit.rs` (7 new tests)
   211→
   212→### Bash glob safety of square brackets
   213→
   214→Square brackets are bash glob characters — `[fx]` matches a single-character filename
   215→from the set {f, x}. Initial concern was that `linker.args+=[-static,-nostdlib]` could
   216→be glob-expanded. Investigation showed this is safe because **bash globs the entire
   217→token**, not just the bracket portion. The token `linker.args+=[-static,-nostdlib]`
   218→would only match a file literally named `linker.args+=-` (or `s`, `t`, etc.), which
   219→will never exist.
   220→
   221→Standalone brackets DO expand:
   222→
   223→```bash
   224→$ echo [fx]          # no f or x file
   225→[fx]
   226→$ touch f
   227→$ echo [fx]          # f exists
   228→f
   229→$ touch x
   230→$ echo [fx]          # both exist
   231→f x
   232→$ rm f
   233→$ echo [fx]          # only x
   234→x
   235→$ rm x
   236→$ echo [fx]          # neither exists, passes through
   237→[fx]
   238→```
   239→
   240→But with a prefix, the whole token fails to match any file:
   241→
   242→```bash
   243→$ touch f x
   244→$ echo [fx]          # standalone: expands
   245→f x
   246→$ echo a=[fx]        # prefixed: no file matches "a=f" or "a=x"
   247→a=[fx]
   248→```
   249→
   250→**Conclusion:** Square brackets are safe unquoted in `tspec ts set key+=[values]`
   251→syntax. No quoting needed for normal use.
   252→
   253→## 20260211 - Orthogonal `ts set`/`add`/`remove` with separate key and value args
   254→
   255→### Problem
   256→
   257→`ts set` takes the entire assignment as a single positional argument (`key=value`),
   258→which forces users to fight the shell:
   259→
   260→```bash
   261→# Scalar — fine
   262→tspec ts set rustc.lto=true
   263→
   264→# Array — bracket/quote gymnastics
   265→tspec ts set 'linker.args=["-static","-nostdlib"]'
   266→
   267→# Append — still awkward
   268→tspec ts set 'linker.args+=-Wl,--gc-sections'
   269→```
   270→
   271→The root cause is two-fold:
   272→
   273→1. **Key and value crammed into one shell token** — any special characters in
   274→   the value (brackets, quotes, spaces) force quoting the entire `key=value`.
   275→2. **Overloaded operators (`=`, `+=`, `-=`) encoded in a string** — three
   276→   fundamentally different operations (replace, append, remove) masquerade as
   277→   modes of one command via string parsing.
   278→
   279→### Design: Orthogonal verbs with separate args
   280→
   281→Split into four commands on two axes:
   282→
   283→| Axis | Command | Purpose |
   284→|---|---|---|
   285→| Field-level | `ts set` | Set scalar or replace entire array |
   286→| Field-level | `ts unset` | Remove entire field (already exists) |
   287→| Item-level | `ts add` | Add items to array (append or insert) |
   288→| Item-level | `ts remove` | Remove items from array (by value or index) |
   289→
   290→Key and value are separate clap positional arguments. Array values are variadic
   291→(each element is its own shell arg — no brackets, no inner quotes).
   292→
   293→### Hyphen-prefixed values and `allow_hyphen_values`
   294→
   295→Values like `-static` and `-nostdlib` start with `-`, which clap normally tries
   296→to parse as flags. The fix is `#[arg(allow_hyphen_values = true)]` on the
   297→`value` field — clap then treats unknown hyphen-prefixed tokens as positional
   298→values. This means **no `--` needed** in normal use:
   299→
   300→```bash
   301→tspec ts set linker.args -static -nostdlib      # just works
   302→tspec ts add linker.args -Wl,--gc-sections      # just works
   303→```
   304→
   305→The only edge case is a value that exactly matches a defined short flag (`-p`,
   306→`-t`, `-i`). In practice no real linker/rustc flag is just `-p` or `-t`, but
   307→`--` remains available as an escape hatch for that theoretical collision.
   308→
   309→### Syntax
   310→
   311→```bash
   312→# === Field-level (set/unset) ===
   313→
   314→# Scalars — just two words
   315→tspec ts set rustc.lto true
   316→tspec ts set cargo.profile release
   317→tspec ts set panic abort
   318→
   319→# Replace entire array — each element is a separate arg
   320→tspec ts set linker.args -static -nostdlib
   321→tspec ts set rustc.build_std core alloc
   322→
   323→# Remove entire field
   324→tspec ts unset linker.args
   325→
   326→# === Item-level (add/remove) ===
   327→
   328→# Append (default add behavior)
   329→tspec ts add linker.args -Wl,--gc-sections
   330→tspec ts add rustc.flags -Cforce-frame-pointers=yes -Clink-dead-code=no
   331→
   332→# Insert at position
   333→tspec ts add -i 0 linker.args -nostdlib
   334→
   335→# Remove by value
   336→tspec ts remove linker.args -static
   337→tspec ts remove linker.args -static -nostdlib
   338→
   339→# Remove by index
   340→tspec ts remove -i 2 linker.args
   341→```
   342→
   343→### The six array operations
   344→
   345→| # | Operation | Command | Example |
   346→|---|---|---|---|
   347→| 1 | Replace all | `ts set` | `ts set linker.args -static -nostdlib` |
   348→| 2 | Clear field | `ts unset` | `ts unset linker.args` |
   349→| 3 | Append items | `ts add` | `ts add linker.args -Wl,--gc-sections` |
   350→| 4 | Insert at position | `ts add -i N` | `ts add -i 0 linker.args -nostdlib` |
   351→| 5 | Remove by value | `ts remove` | `ts remove linker.args -static` |
   352→| 6 | Remove by index | `ts remove -i N` | `ts remove -i 2 linker.args` |
   353→
   354→### Clap definitions
   355→
   356→```rust
   357→/// Set a field (scalar value or replace entire array)
   358→Set {
   359→    /// Field key (e.g., "rustc.lto", "linker.args")
   360→    key: String,
   361→    /// Value(s). For scalars, one value. For arrays, each arg is an element.
   362→    #[arg(required = true, allow_hyphen_values = true)]
   363→    value: Vec<String>,
   364→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
   365→    package: Option<String>,
   366→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
   367→    tspec: Option<String>,
   368→}
   369→
   370→/// Add items to an array field (append by default, or insert at position)
   371→Add {
   372→    /// Field key (must be an array field)
   373→    key: String,
   374→    /// Items to add
   375→    #[arg(required = true, allow_hyphen_values = true)]
   376→    value: Vec<String>,
   377→    /// Insert at this index instead of appending
   378→    #[arg(short = 'i', long = "index")]
   379→    index: Option<usize>,
   380→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
   381→    package: Option<String>,
   382→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
   383→    tspec: Option<String>,
   384→}
   385→
   386→/// Remove items from an array field (by value or by index)
   387→Remove {
   388→    /// Field key (must be an array field)
   389→    key: String,
   390→    /// Items to remove by value (not used with --index)
   391→    #[arg(allow_hyphen_values = true)]
   392→    value: Vec<String>,
   393→    /// Remove item at this index instead of by value
   394→    #[arg(short = 'i', long = "index")]
   395→    index: Option<usize>,
   396→    #[arg(short = 'p', long = "package")]
   397→    package: Option<String>,
   398→    #[arg(short = 't', long = "tspec")]
   399→    tspec: Option<String>,
   400→}
   401→```
   402→
   403→### Validation rules
   404→
   405→- `ts set` on a scalar field: exactly one value required
   406→- `ts set` on an array field: one or more values (replaces entire array)
   407→- `ts add`: key must be an array field (error on scalar)
   408→- `ts add -i N`: index must be ≤ current array length
   409→- `ts remove` without `--index`: at least one value required
   410→- `ts remove -i N` with `--index`: no values expected (index is the selector)
   411→- `ts remove -i N`: index must be < current array length
   412→
   413→### Implementation steps
   414→
   415→#### Step 1: Add `add_items` and `remove_items` to `edit.rs`
   416→
   417→New functions in `src/ts_cmd/edit.rs`:
   418→
   419→- **`add_items(doc, key, values: &[String], index: Option<usize>)`** — like
   420→  `append_field` but takes a `Vec<String>` directly (no string parsing), with
   421→  optional insert-at-index. Deduplicates on append; for insert, adds at position
   422→  without dedup (user explicitly chose the position).
   423→
   424→- **`remove_items_by_value(doc, key, values: &[String])`** — like
   425→  `remove_from_field` but takes `Vec<String>` directly. If array becomes empty,
   426→  removes the field (existing behavior).
   427→
   428→- **`remove_item_by_index(doc, key, index: usize)`** — removes the item at
   429→  the given index. If array becomes empty, removes the field.
   430→
   431→- **`set_field_from_strings(doc, key, values: &[String], kind)`** — for `ts set`.
   432→  Scalars: `values[0]`. Arrays: builds array from all values. Replaces
   433→  `set_field(doc, key, value_str, kind)` which parsed from a single string.
   434→
   435→Keep `parse_array_value()` and `parse_scalar_value()` as internal helpers but
   436→the public API takes `&[String]` — the shell already did the splitting.
   437→
   438→#### Step 2: Add `add_value` and `remove_value` to `ts_cmd/`
   439→
   440→**New file:** `src/ts_cmd/add.rs`
   441→
   442→```rust
   443→pub fn add_value(
   444→    project_root: &Path,
   445→    package: Option<&str>,
   446→    key: &str,
   447→    values: &[String],
   448→    index: Option<usize>,
   449→    tspec: Option<&str>,
   450→) -> Result<()>
   451→```
   452→
   453→- Validate key is an array field (error if scalar)
   454→- Read, parse DocumentMut, call `edit::add_items()`, write back
   455→
   456→**New file:** `src/ts_cmd/remove.rs`

   458→```rust
   459→pub fn remove_value(
   460→    project_root: &Path,
   461→    package: Option<&str>,
   462→    key: &str,
   463→    values: &[String],
   464→    index: Option<usize>,
   465→    tspec: Option<&str>,
   466→) -> Result<()>
   467→```
   468→
   469→- Validate key is an array field (error if scalar)
   470→- If `index` is Some: call `edit::remove_item_by_index()`

> DEVELOPER

Looking good, but I think we need to have explain that a <KEY> has and optional <TABLE> and the syntax is <TABLE>.<KEY>, thoughts?

> AGENT

Good call. The key syntax is implicit right now — users see `rustc.lto` and `linker.args` in the table but there's no explicit explanation that the format is `[TABLE.]FIELD` where the table is optional for top-level fields like `panic` and `strip`.

I'd add a brief note right before the "Supported keys" table in the README, something like:

```
Keys use dotted notation: `TABLE.FIELD` for nested fields (e.g., `rustc.lto`,
`linker.args`) or just `FIELD` for top-level fields (`panic`, `strip`).
```

Short, shows both cases by example, and sits right where someone would look before scanning the table. Want me to add it?

> DEVELOPER

For top level you say optional how do you specify the "global" table for panic & strip, for instance?

> AGENT

You don't — TOML's root has no table name. Top-level fields are just bare keys: `panic`, `strip`. There's no `global.panic` or `.panic` syntax.

So the notation is really two forms, not one with an optional part:

- `FIELD` — top-level (`panic`, `strip`)  
- `TABLE.FIELD` — nested (`rustc.lto`, `linker.args`)

The "Supported keys" table already shows exactly which is which. I'll phrase it that way instead of saying "optional table."

> DEVELOPER

id: toolu_011yEyi5wCRR5AEFuvX6uNtr
```
     1→# CLAUDE.md
     2→
     3→This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.
     4→
     5→## Project Overview
     6→
     7→**tspec** is a spec-driven build system wrapper for Rust that sits on top of cargo. It configures builds at the **package** level (one tspec per Cargo package) via translation spec files (TOML-based) with support for target triples, compiler flags, linker options, and high-level build options like panic strategies and symbol stripping. For per-crate control, use separate packages in a workspace.
     8→
     9→## Build Commands
    10→
    11→Ensure a recent tspec is installed:
    12→```bash
    13→cargo install --path .         # Install from local source
    14→```
    15→
    16→Development workflow:
    17→```bash
    18→tspec test -p tspec            # Run tests
    19→tspec clean                    # Clean build artifacts
    20→tspec clippy                   # Run lints
    21→tspec fmt --check              # Check formatting
    22→```
    23→
    24→## Architecture
    25→
    26→### Execute Trait Pattern
    27→
    28→Commands implement the `Execute` trait:
    29→
    30→```rust
    31→pub trait Execute {
    32→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode>;
    33→}
    34→
    35→// Helper for simple cargo passthroughs
    36→pub fn execute_cargo_subcommand(subcommand: &str, args: &[OsString], project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode>
    37→```
    38→
    39→- **Simple passthroughs** (Clean, Clippy, Fmt): Use `execute_cargo_subcommand()` helper
    40→- **Custom commands** (Build, Run, Test, Compare, Install, Version): Implement own logic
    41→
    42→Command structs derive `clap::Args` and are used directly in the `Commands` enum:
    43→```rust
    44→enum Commands {
    45→    Clean(CleanCmd),
    46→    Test(TestCmd),
    47→    // ...
    48→}
    49→```
    50→
    51→### Key Modules
    52→
    53→- `cmd/` - Command implementations (one file per command)
    54→  - `mod.rs` - Execute trait, execute_cargo_subcommand helper, re-exports
    55→  - `build.rs`, `clean.rs`, `clippy.rs`, `compare.rs`, `fmt.rs`, `install.rs`, `run.rs`, `test.rs`, `version.rs` - Individual commands
    56→- `cli.rs` - Clap CLI definitions
    57→- `types.rs` - Spec types (CargoConfig, RustcConfig, LinkerConfig)
    58→- `tspec.rs` - Spec loading/saving/hashing, `copy_spec_snapshot()` for byte-for-byte backups
    59→- `cargo_build.rs` - Package build orchestration with spec application
    60→- `workspace.rs` - Workspace package discovery
    61→- `all.rs` - Batch operations (build_all, test_all, run_all)
    62→- `ts_cmd/edit.rs` - `toml_edit` helpers: field registry, `set_field()`, `unset_field()`, validation
    63→- `ts_cmd/unset.rs` - `ts unset` command
    64→
    65→### Three Write Strategies
    66→
    67→| Operation | Strategy | Why |
    68→|---|---|---|
    69→| load, hash, build | serde (`toml`) | Need typed `Spec` struct for build logic |
    70→| set, unset | `toml_edit` (`DocumentMut`) | Surgical edit preserving comments/formatting |
    71→| backup, restore, new -f | raw `fs::copy` | Exact byte-for-byte preservation |
    72→
    73→### Translation Spec Structure
    74→
    75→Specs are TOML files (`*.ts.toml`) with three sections:
    76→- `[cargo]` - profile, target_triple, target_json, unstable flags
    77→- `[rustc]` - opt_level, panic, lto, codegen_units, build_std, flags
    78→- `[linker]` - args, version_script
    79→
    80→## Conventions
    81→
    82→- **Rust Edition:** 2024
    83→- **Commit style:** Conventional commits (feat:, docs:, refactor:)
    84→- **Naming:** POP (Plain Old Package) refers to single-package projects (no workspace); tspec treats them as trivial workspaces
    85→- **Granularity:** tspec operates at the Cargo package level, not the crate level. "Package" = directory with Cargo.toml. A package may contain multiple crates (targets), but they all share one tspec.
    86→- **Markdown refs:** Multiple references use `[1],[2]` not `[1,2]` or `[1][2]` (both break in markdown)
    87→
    88→## Workflow
    89→
    90→**Before committing, run verification:**
    91→```bash
    92→tspec test -p tspec
    93→tspec clippy
    94→tspec fmt --check
    95→```
    96→
    97→**After committing code, remind about .claude/ files:**
    98→```
    99→Committed abc123.
   100→
   101→Remember to commit .claude/ session files.
   102→```
   103→
   104→**On next prompt after a commit+reminder:** Check `git log -1 --name-only` to see if `.claude/` was included in a commit after the code commit. If not, ask: "Did you forget to commit .claude sessions?"
   105→
   106→## Git Operations Claude Cannot Perform
   107→
   108→Due to circular references with `.claude/` session files (which record Claude's behavior), Claude cannot perform these git operations:
   109→
   110→- `git checkout` - switching branches affects session files
   111→- `git merge` - merging affects session files
   112→- `git rebase` - rebasing affects session files
   113→- Amending commits with `.claude/` files
   114→
   115→The user must perform these operations manually. Claude can only make commits (excluding `.claude/` files) and remind the user to handle the rest.
   116→
```

```
     1→# CLAUDE.md
     2→
     3→This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.
     4→
     5→## Project Overview
     6→
     7→**tspec** is a spec-driven build system wrapper for Rust that sits on top of cargo. It configures builds at the **package** level (one tspec per Cargo package) via translation spec files (TOML-based) with support for target triples, compiler flags, linker options, and high-level build options like panic strategies and symbol stripping. For per-crate control, use separate packages in a workspace.
     8→
     9→## Build Commands — Dogfood tspec
    10→
    11→**Always use `tspec` itself for development.** Never use bare `cargo test`, `cargo clippy`, `cargo fmt`, or `cargo install` — use the tspec equivalents. This is how we catch real-world issues.
    12→
    13→```bash
    14→tspec test -p tspec            # Run tests (NEVER cargo test)
    15→tspec clippy                   # Run lints (NEVER cargo clippy)
    16→tspec fmt --check              # Check formatting (NEVER cargo fmt --check)
    17→tspec install --path .         # Install from local source (NEVER cargo install)
    18→```
    19→
    20→**After code changes pass tests, immediately install the new version** with `tspec install --path .` so subsequent commands use the latest binary. Do not defer installation — the sooner we run the real binary, the sooner we catch issues.
    21→
    22→## Architecture
    23→
    24→### Execute Trait Pattern
    25→
    26→Commands implement the `Execute` trait:
    27→
    28→```rust
    29→pub trait Execute {
    30→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode>;
    31→}
    32→
    33→// Helper for simple cargo passthroughs
    34→pub fn execute_cargo_subcommand(subcommand: &str, args: &[OsString], project_root: &Path) -> Result<ExitCode>
    35→```
    36→
    37→- **Simple passthroughs** (Clean, Clippy, Fmt): Use `execute_cargo_subcommand()` helper
    38→- **Custom commands** (Build, Run, Test, Compare, Install, Version): Implement own logic
    39→
    40→Command structs derive `clap::Args` and are used directly in the `Commands` enum:
    41→```rust
    42→enum Commands {
    43→    Clean(CleanCmd),
    44→    Test(TestCmd),
    45→    // ...
    46→}
    47→```
    48→
    49→### Key Modules
    50→
    51→- `cmd/` - Command implementations (one file per command)
    52→  - `mod.rs` - Execute trait, execute_cargo_subcommand helper, re-exports
    53→  - `build.rs`, `clean.rs`, `clippy.rs`, `compare.rs`, `fmt.rs`, `install.rs`, `run.rs`, `test.rs`, `version.rs` - Individual commands
    54→- `cli.rs` - Clap CLI definitions
    55→- `types.rs` - Spec types (CargoConfig, RustcConfig, LinkerConfig)
    56→- `tspec.rs` - Spec loading/saving/hashing, `copy_spec_snapshot()` for byte-for-byte backups
    57→- `cargo_build.rs` - Package build orchestration with spec application
    58→- `workspace.rs` - Workspace package discovery
    59→- `all.rs` - Batch operations (build_all, test_all, run_all)
    60→- `ts_cmd/edit.rs` - `toml_edit` helpers: field registry, `set_field()`, `unset_field()`, `add_items()`, `remove_items_by_value()`, `remove_item_by_index()`, validation
    61→- `ts_cmd/set.rs` - `ts set` command (scalar or replace entire array)
    62→- `ts_cmd/unset.rs` - `ts unset` command (remove field entirely)
    63→- `ts_cmd/add.rs` - `ts add` command (append or insert at position)
    64→- `ts_cmd/remove.rs` - `ts remove` command (by value or by index)
    65→
    66→### Three Write Strategies
    67→
    68→| Operation | Strategy | Why |
    69→|---|---|---|
    70→| load, hash, build | serde (`toml`) | Need typed `Spec` struct for build logic |
    71→| set, unset, add, remove | `toml_edit` (`DocumentMut`) | Surgical edit preserving comments/formatting |
    72→| backup, restore, new -f | raw `fs::copy` | Exact byte-for-byte preservation |
    73→
    74→### Translation Spec Structure
    75→
    76→Specs are TOML files (`*.ts.toml`) with three sections:
    77→- `[cargo]` - profile, target_triple, target_json, unstable flags
    78→- `[rustc]` - opt_level, panic, lto, codegen_units, build_std, flags
    79→- `[linker]` - args, version_script
    80→
    81→## Conventions
    82→
    83→- **Rust Edition:** 2024
    84→- **Commit style:** Conventional commits (feat:, docs:, refactor:)
    85→- **Naming:** POP (Plain Old Package) refers to single-package projects (no workspace); tspec treats them as trivial workspaces
    86→- **Granularity:** tspec operates at the Cargo package level, not the crate level. "Package" = directory with Cargo.toml. A package may contain multiple crates (targets), but they all share one tspec.
    87→- **Markdown refs:** Multiple references use `[1],[2]` not `[1,2]` or `[1][2]` (both break in markdown)
    88→
    89→## Workflow
    90→
    91→**After code changes, run verification and install immediately:**
    92→```bash
    93→tspec test -p tspec
    94→tspec clippy
    95→tspec fmt --check
    96→tspec install --path .         # Install ASAP so we dogfood the new binary
    97→```
    98→
    99→**After committing code, remind about .claude/ files:**
   100→```
   101→Committed abc123.
   102→
   103→Remember to commit .claude/ session files.
   104→```
   105→
   106→**On next prompt after a commit+reminder:** Check `git log -1 --name-only` to see if `.claude/` was included in a commit after the code commit. If not, ask: "Did you forget to commit .claude sessions?"
   107→
   108→## Git Operations Claude Cannot Perform
   109→
   110→Due to circular references with `.claude/` session files (which record Claude's behavior), Claude cannot perform these git operations:
   111→
   112→- `git checkout` - switching branches affects session files
   113→- `git merge` - merging affects session files
   114→- `git rebase` - rebasing affects session files
   115→- Amending commits with `.claude/` files
   116→
   117→The user must perform these operations manually. Claude can only make commits (excluding `.claude/` files) and remind the user to handle the rest.
   118→
```

Nice tweak — test with old binary, install, then test again with the new binary to catch issues early. The double-test-with-install-in-between pattern makes sense: first validates the code, second validates the installed binary itself.

Looks good to me. Ready to commit when you are.
